 6   BE IT ENACTED BY THE LEGISLATURE OF THE STATE OF WASHINGTON:


 7        NEW SECTION.  Sec. 1.  The legislature finds that the purpose of
 8   the transportation system is to support the mobility needs of
 9   Washington residents, as well as to sustain and foster the economic
10   activity and growth of the state. The legislature recognizes that the
11   transportation system has pressing near, mid, and long-term needs
12   that necessitate reliance on reliable funding resources, as well as
13   the  efficient  use  of  those  resources.  The  legislature  further
14   recognizes that the production, maintenance, and utilization of
15   transportation resources across the state is inherently a complex,
16   multifaceted issue. The legislature therefore intends to address
17   these resources needs in a comprehensive manner. As such, the
18   legislature's purpose in enacting this legislation is to address the
19   complex production, maintenance, and utilization of transportation
20   resources in Washington to achieve both short-term investment needs
21   and  provide  a  long-range  vision  for  transportation  system
22   development.


23                           PART I: MOTOR VEHICLE FUEL TAX


24        Sec. 101.  RCW 82.38.030 and 2015 3rd sp.s. c 44 s 103 are each
25   amended to read as follows:
26        (1) There is levied and imposed upon fuel licensees a tax at the
27   rate of ((twenty-three)) 23 cents per gallon of fuel.
28        (2) Beginning July 1, 2003, an additional and cumulative tax rate
29   of five cents per gallon of fuel is imposed on fuel licensees. This
30   subsection    (2) expires when the bonds issued for transportation 2003
31   projects are retired.
32        (3) Beginning July 1, 2005, an additional and cumulative tax rate
33   of three cents per gallon of fuel is imposed on fuel licensees.
34        (4) Beginning July 1, 2006, an additional and cumulative tax rate
35   of three cents per gallon of fuel is imposed on fuel licensees.


                                             p. 2                          ESSB 5801.SL
 1        (5) Beginning July 1, 2007, an additional and cumulative tax rate
 2   of two cents per gallon of fuel is imposed on fuel licensees.
 3        (6) Beginning July 1, 2008, an additional and cumulative tax rate
 4   of one and one-half cents per gallon of fuel is imposed on fuel
 5   licensees.
 6        (7) Beginning August 1, 2015, an additional and cumulative tax
 7   rate of seven cents per gallon of fuel is imposed on fuel licensees.
 8        (8) Beginning July 1, 2016, an additional and cumulative tax rate
 9   of four and nine-tenths cents per gallon of fuel is imposed on fuel
10   licensees.
11        (9) Beginning July 1, 2025, an additional and cumulative tax rate
12   of six cents per gallon of fuel is imposed on fuel licensees.
13        (10)   Beginning July 1, 2025, an additional and cumulative tax
14   rate of three cents per gallon of special fuel is imposed on fuel
15   licensees.
16        (11)   Beginning July 1, 2027, an additional and cumulative tax
17   rate of three cents per gallon of special fuel is imposed on fuel
18   licensees.
19        (12)(a)   Beginning July 1, 2026, the fuel tax rates imposed under
20   subsections     (1)   through    (9)   of this section must be increased
21   annually by two percent and the resulting fuel tax rate must be
22   rounded to the nearest one-thousandth of $1.
23        (b)   Beginning July 1, 2028, the fuel tax rate imposed under
24   subsections    (10) and (11) of this section must be increased annually
25   by two percent and the resulting fuel tax rate must be rounded to the
26   nearest one-thousandth of $1.
27        (13) Taxes are imposed when:
28        (a) Fuel is removed in this state from a terminal if the fuel is
29   removed at the rack unless the removal is by a licensed supplier or
30   distributor for direct delivery to a destination outside of the
31   state, or the removal is by a fuel supplier for direct delivery to an
32   international fuel tax agreement licensee under RCW 82.38.320;
33        (b) Fuel is removed in this state from a refinery if either of
34   the following applies:
35        (i) The removal is by bulk transfer and the refiner or the owner
36   of the fuel immediately before the removal is not a licensed
37   supplier; or
38        (ii) The removal is at the refinery rack unless the removal is to
39   a  licensed  supplier  or  distributor  for  direct  delivery  to  a
40   destination outside of the state, or the removal is to a licensed
                                             p. 3                          ESSB 5801.SL
 1   supplier for direct delivery to an international fuel tax agreement
 2   licensee under RCW 82.38.320;
 3        (c) Fuel enters into this state for sale, consumption, use, or
 4   storage, unless the fuel enters this state for direct delivery to an
 5   international fuel tax agreement licensee under RCW                  82.38.320, if
 6   either of the following applies:
 7        (i) The entry is by bulk transfer and the importer is not a
 8   licensed supplier; or
 9        (ii) The entry is not by bulk transfer;
10        (d) Fuel enters this state by means outside the bulk transfer-
11   terminal system and is delivered directly to a licensed terminal
12   unless the owner is a licensed distributor or supplier;
13        (e) Fuel is sold or removed in this state to an unlicensed entity
14   unless there was a prior taxable removal, entry, or sale of the fuel;
15        (f) Blended fuel is removed or sold in this state by the blender
16   of the fuel. The number of gallons of blended fuel subject to tax is
17   the difference between the total number of gallons of blended fuel
18   removed or sold and the number of gallons of previously taxed fuel
19   used to produce the blended fuel;
20        (g) Dyed special fuel is used on a highway, as authorized by the
21   internal revenue code, unless the use is exempt from the fuel tax;
22        (h) Dyed special fuel is held for sale, sold, used, or is
23   intended to be used in violation of this chapter;
24        (i) Special fuel purchased by an international fuel tax agreement
25   licensee under RCW 82.38.320 is used on a highway; and
26        (j)  Fuel  is  sold  by  a  licensed  fuel  supplier  to  a  fuel
27   distributor or fuel blender and the fuel is not removed from the bulk
28   transfer-terminal system.


29        Sec. 102.  RCW 82.38.075 and 2014 c 216 s 202 are each amended to
30   read as follows:
31        (1) To encourage the use of nonpolluting fuels, an annual license
32   fee in lieu of the tax imposed by RCW            82.38.030 is imposed upon the
33   use of liquefied natural gas, compressed natural gas, or propane used
34   in any motor vehicle. The annual license fee must be based upon the
35   following schedule and formula:

36                             VEHICLE TONNAGE (GVW)       FEE
37                                  0  -   6,000           $ 45
38                               6,001 -  10,000           $ 45

                                              p. 4                          ESSB 5801.SL
       1                        10,001 -  18,000            $ 80

       2                        18,001 -  28,000            $110

       3                        28,001 -  36,000            $150

       4                        36,001  and above           $250

 5        (2) To determine the annual license fee for a registration year,
 6   the appropriate dollar amount in the schedule is multiplied by the
 7   cumulative fuel tax rate per gallon of special fuel effective on July
 8   1st of the preceding calendar year and the product is divided by 12
 9   cents.   The annual license fee must be rounded to the nearest five
10   cents.
11        (3) The department, in addition to the resulting fee, must charge
12   an additional fee of        ((five dollars))      $5 as a handling charge for
13   each license issued.
14        (4) The vehicle tonnage fee must be prorated so the annual
15   license will correspond with the staggered vehicle licensing system.
16        (5) A decal or other identifying device issued upon payment of
17   the annual fee must be displayed as prescribed by the department as
18   authority to purchase this fuel.
19        (6) Persons selling or dispensing natural gas or propane may not
20   sell or dispense this fuel for their own use or the use of others
21   into tanks of vehicles powered by this fuel which do not display a
22   valid decal or other identifying device.
23        (7)  Commercial  motor  vehicles  registered  in  a  foreign
24   jurisdiction under the provisions of the international registration
25   plan are subject to the annual fee.
26        (8) Motor vehicles registered in a foreign jurisdiction, except
27   those registered under the international registration plan under
28   chapter 46.87 RCW, are exempt from this section.
29        (9) Vehicles registered in jurisdictions outside the state of
30   Washington are exempt from this section.
31        (10) Any person selling or dispensing liquefied natural gas,
32   compressed natural gas, or propane into the tank of a motor vehicle
33   powered by this fuel, except as prescribed in this chapter, is
34   subject to the penalty provisions of this chapter.

13   is levied and must be collected in an amount equal to the value of
14   the article used, value of the digital good or digital code used,
15   value of the extended warranty used, or value of the service used by
16   the taxpayer, multiplied by the applicable rates in effect for the
17   retail sales tax under RCW 82.08.020.
18        (b) In the case of a seller required to collect use tax from the
19   purchaser, the tax must be collected in an amount equal to the
20   purchase price multiplied by the applicable rate in effect for the
21   retail sales tax under RCW 82.08.020.
22        (5) For purposes of the tax imposed in this section, "person"
23   includes anyone within the definition of "buyer," "purchaser," and
24   "consumer" in RCW 82.08.010.
25        (6)(a) Beginning July 1, 2026, the tax imposed in this section at
26   the rate provided in RCW           82.08.020(4)     applies to the use of a
27   recreational vessel at the time that it is first used in this state
28   by the consumer.
29        (b) The revenue collected under this subsection must be deposited
30   in the multimodal transportation account created in RCW 47.66.070.
31        (c) For purposes of this subsection, "recreational vessel" means
32   a vessel as defined in RCW         88.02.310    that is subject to watercraft
33   excise tax under chapter 82.49 RCW.

                                            p. 25                         ESSB 5801.SL
 1   of the responsible individuals. For purposes of this subsection,

 4   department may presume that an entity is insolvent if the entity
 5   refuses to disclose to the department the nature of its assets and
 6   liabilities.

 8   state and local trust fund taxes.
 9        (3)(a) For a responsible individual who is the current or a
10   former chief executive or chief financial officer, liability under

20   or chief financial officer is liable under this section only for

 1        (6) Any person having been issued a notice of assessment under

24   entity that is managed or owned in whole or in part by an entity that

29   joint ventures in which one or more of the partners or parties are
30   also limited liability business entities, and limited partnerships in

32   business entities.

35   that  the  term  only  includes  members  of  member-managed  limited
36   liability companies.

38   corporation, including the president, vice president, secretary, and
39   treasurer.

                                            p. 31                          ESSB 5801.SL
 1        (3)(a) The department of transportation is responsible for all
 2   actions related to the operation and administration of speed safety
 3   camera systems in state highway work zones including, but not limited
 4   to, the procurement and administration of contracts necessary for the
 5   implementation of speed safety camera systems, the mailing of notices
 6   of infraction, and the development and maintenance of a public-facing
 7   website for the purpose of educating the traveling public about the
 8   use of speed safety camera systems in state highway work zones. Prior
 9   to the use of a speed safety camera system to capture a violation
10   established in this section for enforcement purposes, the department
11   of transportation, in consultation with the Washington state patrol,

18   this section including, but not limited to, notice of infraction
19   verification and issuance authorization, and determining which types
20   of emergency vehicles are exempt from being issued notices of
21   infraction under this section. Prior to the use of a speed safety
22   camera system to capture a violation established in this section for

24   with the department of transportation, department of licensing,
25   office  of  administrative  hearings,  Washington  traffic  safety

28   necessary steps to implement this section.
29        (c) When establishing rules under this subsection                   (3), the
30   department of transportation and the Washington state patrol may also
31   consult with other public and private agencies that have an interest
32   in the use of speed safety camera systems in state highway work
33   zones.
34        (4)(a) No person may drive a vehicle in a state highway work zone
35   at a speed greater than that allowed by traffic control devices.
36        (b) A notice of infraction may only be issued under this section
37   if a speed safety camera system captures a speed violation in a state
38   highway work zone when workers are present.



                                            p. 32                         ESSB 5801.SL
 1        (5) The penalty for a speed safety camera system violation is:
 2   (a) (($0)) $125 for the first violation; and (b) $248 for the second
 3   violation, and for each violation thereafter.
 4        (6) During the 30-day period after the first speed safety camera
 5   system is put in place, the department is required to conduct a
 6   public awareness campaign to inform the public of the use of speed
 7   safety camera systems in state highway work zones.
 8        (7)(a) A notice of infraction issued under this section may be
 9   mailed to the registered owner of the vehicle within 30 days of the
10   violation,  or  to  the  renter  of  a  vehicle  within  30  days  of
11   establishing the renter's name and address. The law enforcement
12   officer issuing the notice of infraction shall include with it a
13   certificate  or  facsimile  thereof,  based  upon  inspection  of
14   photographs, microphotographs, or electronic images produced by a
15   speed safety camera stating the facts supporting the notice of
16   infraction. This certificate or facsimile is prima facie evidence of
17   the facts contained in it and is admissible in a proceeding charging
18   a violation under this section. The photographs, microphotographs, or
19   electronic images evidencing the violation must be available for
20   inspection and admission into evidence in a proceeding to adjudicate
21   the liability for the violation.
22        (b) A notice of infraction represents a determination that an
23   infraction has been committed, and the determination will be final
24   unless contested as provided under this section.
25        (c) A person receiving a notice of infraction based on evidence
26   detected by a speed safety camera system must, within 30 days of
27   receiving  the  notice  of  infraction:           (i)   ((Except  for  a  first
28   violation under subsection         (5)(a)   of this section, remit))          Remit
29   payment in the amount of the penalty assessed for the violation; (ii)
30   contest the determination that the infraction occurred by following
31   the instructions on the notice of infraction; or             (iii) admit to the
32   infraction but request a hearing to explain mitigating circumstances
33   surrounding the infraction.
34        (d) If a person fails to respond to a notice of infraction, a
35   final order shall be entered finding that the person committed the
36   infraction and assessing monetary penalties required under subsection
37   (5)(((b))) of this section.
38        (e) If a person contests the determination that the infraction
39   occurred or requests a mitigation hearing, the notice of infraction


                                            p. 33                         ESSB 5801.SL
 1   shall be referred to the office of administrative hearings for
 2   adjudication consistent with chapter 34.05 RCW.
 3        (f) At a hearing to contest an infraction, the agency issuing the
 4   infraction has the burden of proving, by a preponderance of the
 5   evidence, that the infraction was committed.
 6        (g) A person may request a payment plan at any time for the
 7   payment of any penalty or other monetary obligation associated with
 8   an infraction under this section. The agency issuing the infraction
 9   shall provide information about how to submit evidence of inability
10   to pay, how to obtain a payment plan, and that failure to pay or
11   enter into a payment plan may result in collection action or
12   nonrenewal of the vehicle registration. The office of administrative
13   hearings may authorize a payment plan if it determines that a person
14   is not able to pay the monetary obligation, and it may modify a
15   payment plan at any time.
16        (8)(a) Speed safety camera systems may only take photographs,
17   microphotographs, or electronic images of the vehicle and vehicle
18   license plate and only while a speed violation is occurring. The
19   photograph, microphotograph, or electronic image must not reveal the
20   face of the driver or any passengers in the vehicle. The department
21   of transportation shall consider installing speed safety camera
22   systems in a manner that minimizes the impact of camera flash on
23   drivers.
24        (b) The registered owner of a vehicle is responsible for a
25   traffic infraction under RCW         46.63.030 unless the registered owner
26   overcomes the presumption in RCW           46.63.075 or, in the case of a
27   rental car business, satisfies the conditions under                 (f) of this
28   subsection.  If  appropriate  under  the  circumstances,  a  renter
29   identified under      (f)(i) of this subsection is responsible for the
30   traffic infraction.
31        (c) Notwithstanding any other provision of law, all photographs,
32   microphotographs, or electronic images, or any other personally
33   identifying data prepared under this section are for the exclusive
34   use of the Washington state patrol and department of transportation
35   in the discharge of duties under this section and are not open to the
36   public and may not be used in court in a pending action or proceeding
37   unless the action or proceeding relates to a speed violation under
38   this  section.  This  data  may  be  used  in  administrative  appeal
39   proceedings relative to a violation under this section.


                                           p. 34                          ESSB 5801.SL
 1        (d) All locations where speed safety camera systems are used must
 2   be clearly marked before activation of the camera system by placing
 3   signs in locations that clearly indicate to a driver that they are
 4   entering a state highway work zone where posted speed limits are
 5   monitored by a speed safety camera system. Additionally, where
 6   feasible and constructive, radar speed feedback signs will be placed
 7   in advance of the speed safety camera system to assist drivers in
 8   complying with posted speed limits. Signs placed in these locations
 9   must follow the specifications and guidelines under the manual of
10   uniform traffic control devices for streets and highways as adopted
11   by the department of transportation under chapter 47.36 RCW.
12        (e) Imposition of a penalty for a speed violation detected
13   through the use of speed safety camera systems shall not be deemed a
14   conviction as defined in RCW 46.25.010, and shall not be part of the
15   registered owner's driving record under RCW           46.52.101 and     46.52.120.
16   Additionally, infractions generated by the use of speed safety camera
17   systems under this section shall be processed in the same manner as
18   parking infractions, including for the purposes of RCW 46.16A.120 and
19   46.20.270(2).
20        (f) If the registered owner of the vehicle is a rental car
21   business, the department of transportation shall, before a notice of
22   infraction may be issued under this section, provide a written notice
23   to the rental car business that a notice of infraction may be issued
24   to the rental car business if the rental car business does not,
25   within 30 days of receiving the written notice, provide to the
26   issuing agency by return mail:
27        (i)(A) A statement under oath stating the name and known mailing
28   address of the individual driving or renting the vehicle when the
29   speed violation occurred;
30        (B) A statement under oath that the business is unable to
31   determine who was driving or renting the vehicle at the time the
32   speed violation occurred because the vehicle was stolen at the time
33   of  the  violation.  A  statement  provided  under  this  subsection
34   (8)(f)(i)(B) must be accompanied by a copy of a filed police report
35   regarding the vehicle theft; or
36        (C) In lieu of identifying the vehicle operator, payment of the
37   applicable penalty.
38        (ii)  Timely  mailing  of  a  statement  to  the  department  of
39   transportation relieves a rental car business of any liability under
40   this chapter for the notice of infraction.
                                            p. 35                         ESSB 5801.SL
 1        (9) Revenue generated from the deployment of speed safety camera
 2   systems must be deposited into the highway safety fund and first used
 3   exclusively for the operating and administrative costs under this
 4   section. The operation of speed safety camera systems is intended to
 5   increase safety in state highway work zones by changing driver
 6   behavior.  Consequently,  any  revenue  generated  that  exceeds  the
 7   operating  and  administrative  costs  under  this  section  must  be
 8   distributed for the purpose of traffic safety including, but not
 9   limited to, driver training education and local DUI emphasis patrols.
10        (10)   The   Washington   state   patrol   and   department   of
11   transportation, in collaboration with the Washington traffic safety
12   commission, must report to the transportation committees of the
13   legislature by July 1, 2025, and biennially thereafter, on the data
14   and efficacy of speed safety camera system use in state highway work
15   zones. The final report due on July 1, 2029, must include a
16   recommendation on whether or not to continue such speed safety camera
17   system use beyond June 30, 2030.
18        (11) For the purposes of this section:
19        (a) "Speed safety camera system" means employing the use of speed
20   measuring devices and cameras synchronized to automatically record
21   one  or  more  sequenced  photographs,  microphotographs,  or  other
22   electronic images of a motor vehicle that exceeds a posted state
23   highway work zone speed limit as detected by the speed measuring
24   devices.
25        (b) "State highway work zone" means an area of any highway with
26   construction,  maintenance,  utility  work,  or  incident  response
27   activities authorized by the department of transportation. A state
28   highway work zone is identified by the placement of temporary traffic
29   control  devices  that  may  include  signs,  channelizing  devices,
30   barriers,  pavement  markings,  and/or  work  vehicles  with  warning
31   lights. It extends from the first warning sign or high intensity
32   rotating, flashing, oscillating, or strobe lights on a vehicle to the
33   end road work sign or the last temporary traffic control device or
34   vehicle.
35        (12) This section expires June 30, 2030.

 8        Sec. 403.  RCW 47.60.826 and 2023 c 429 s 2 are each amended to
 9   read as follows:
10        (1)(a) The department shall contract for the acquisition of ((up
11   to)) five    or more new hybrid diesel-electric ferry vessels that can
12   carry up to      ((144))    160 vehicles, using a one or two contract
13   procurement approach to potentially accelerate vessel delivery.
14        (b) The Washington state ferries shall make available the design
15   for the   ((144))   160 vehicle hybrid electric Olympic class vessel to
16   potential bidders. Incentives may be awarded by the department to
17   bidders who offer design modifications that:
18        (i) Lower the minimum number of crew needed to staff the vessel
19   in accordance with United States coast guard requirements;
20        (ii) Incorporate materials, technologies, or other features that
21   lower life-cycle maintenance and operations costs;
22        (iii) Accelerate the proposed delivery schedule; or
23        (iv) Make other improvements determined to be beneficial by the
24   department. The Washington state ferries may allow for exceptions of
25   the ((144)) 160 vehicle capacity of the vessel design in cases where
26   efficiencies outlined in (b)(i) or (ii) of this subsection are met.
27        (2)(a) The contract or contracts must be for a minimum of two
28   vessels, with options for ((up to five vessels in total)) additional
29   vessels, and are exempt from the requirements set forth in RCW
30   47.60.810 through 47.60.824.
31        (b)  The  contract  or  contracts  may  employ  the  following
32   procurement methods:
33        (i) Design-build procedure as authorized under chapter 39.10 RCW;
34        (ii) Design-bid-build as authorized under chapter 39.04 RCW or an
35   equivalent  process  allowed  in  statute  as  determined  by  the
36   department; or
37        (iii) Lease with an option to buy in accordance with RCW
38   47.60.010. The terms of any plan to pursue a lease with an option to
39   buy agreement must be approved by the governor and appropriate
                                            p. 45                         ESSB 5801.SL
 1   committees of the legislature and are subject to the availability of
 2   amounts appropriated for this specific purpose.
 3        (c) To the extent possible, the department shall establish and
 4   apply evaluation criteria beyond low price to meet best value
 5   objectives.
 6        (d) The department must award a credit of 13 percent of the bid
 7   price for bid proposals for vessels constructed in the state of
 8   Washington, which must be adjusted to reflect the proportion of the
 9   construction of the vessels that occurs within the state. This credit
10   represents the:
11        (i)  Amount  of  economic  and  revenue  loss  to  the  state  of
12   Washington from constructing vessels outside the state of Washington,
13   as indicated by the Washington institute for public policy study
14   regarding Washington state ferry vessel procurement dated December
15   2016; and
16        (ii) Additional costs of transport, potential delay, and owner
17   oversight incurred for construction at shipyards located outside the
18   state of Washington.
19        (e)  The  department  must  require  that  contractors  meet  the
20   requirements of RCW        39.04.320 regarding apprenticeships or other
21   state law or federal law equivalents, where such equivalents exist.
22        (f)  The  department  must  require  that  contractors  meet  the
23   requirements of     chapter   90.48 RCW regarding water pollution control
24   or other state law or federal law equivalents, where such equivalents
25   exist.
26        (3)  For  contracts  eligible  for  the  use  of  federal  funds,
27   contractors  must  comply  with  federal  disadvantaged  business
28   enterprise targets as outlined by the federal agency awarding funds.
29        (4) Contractors located in the state of Washington must meet the
30   requirements  of  RCW        47.60.835,  the  small  business  enterprise
31   enforceable goals program.
32        (5) The department shall employ third-party experts that report
33   to the Washington state ferries to serve as a supplementary resource.
34   The third-party experts contracted by the Washington state ferries
35   shall:
36        (a)  Perform  project  quality  oversight  and  report  to  the
37   transportation committees of the legislature and the office of
38   financial management on a semiannual basis on project schedule,
39   risks, and project budget;
40        (b) Assist with the management of change order requests;
                                            p. 46                          ESSB 5801.SL
 1        (c) Advise on contract and technical matters; and
 2        (d) Possess knowledge of and experience with inland waterways,
 3   Puget Sound vessel operations, the propulsion system of the new
 4   vessels, and Washington state ferries operations.


 5        NEW SECTION.  Sec. 404.  Nothing in section 403 of this act shall
 6   be construed to apply to, or otherwise interfere with, vessel
 7   procurements underway prior to the effective date of section 403 of
 8   this act.


 9        NEW SECTION.  Sec. 405.  A new section is added to chapter 47.60
10   RCW to read as follows:
11        The  Washington  state  ferries  shall  implement  cost  recovery
12   mechanisms to recoup at least three percent in credit card and other

14   imposed under RCW       47.60.290 and      47.60.315. As part of the cost

16   transaction fees incurred through credit card transactions. The
17   Washington state ferries must notify customers of the fee at the
18   point of sale and itemize the fee on customer receipts. Costs
19   recovered under this section may not be considered revenue for the

25   read as follows:
26        (1) The commission shall fix the rates of toll and other charges
27   for all toll bridges built under this chapter that are financed
28   primarily by bonds issued by the state. Subject to RCW 47.46.090, the
29   commission may impose and modify toll charges from time to time as
30   conditions warrant.      However, except for publicly or privately owned
31   or operated school buses, the commission may not exempt publicly or
32   privately owned or operated transit buses, vans, and ride share
33   vehicles, and must modify tolling provisions accordingly by October
34   1, 2025.
35        (2) In establishing toll charges, the commission shall give due
36   consideration to any required costs for operating and maintaining the
37   toll bridge or toll bridges, including the cost of insurance, and to


                                            p. 48                          ESSB 5801.SL
 1   any amount required by law to meet the redemption of bonds and
 2   interest payments on them.
 3        (3) The toll charges must be imposed in amounts sufficient to:
 4        (a) Provide annual revenue sufficient to provide for annual
 5   operating  and  maintenance  expenses,  except  as  provided  in  RCW
 6   47.56.245;
 7        (b) Make payments required under RCW             47.56.165 and      47.46.140,

 9   on bonds issued for any particular toll bridge or toll bridges; and
10        (c) Repay the motor vehicle fund under RCW 47.46.110,               47.56.165,
11   and 47.46.140.
12        (4) The bond principal and interest payments, including repayment
13   of the motor vehicle fund for amounts transferred from that fund to
14   provide for such principal and interest payments, constitute a first
15   direct and exclusive charge and lien on all tolls and other revenues
16   from the toll bridge concerned, subject to operating and maintenance
17   expenses.


18        Sec. 502.  RCW 47.56.850 and 2009 c 498 s 15 are each amended to
19   read as follows:
20        (1)  Unless  these  powers  are  otherwise  delegated  by  the
21   legislature, the transportation commission is the tolling authority
22   for the state. The tolling authority shall:
23        (a) Set toll rates, establish appropriate exemptions, if any, and
24   make adjustments as conditions warrant on eligible toll facilities.
25   However, except for publicly or privately owned or operated school
26   buses, the commission may not exempt publicly or privately owned or
27   operated transit buses, vans, and ride share vehicles from tolls on
28   bridges, and must modify tolling provisions accordingly by October 1,
29   2025;
30        (b) Review toll collection policies, toll operations policies,
31   and toll revenue expenditures on the eligible toll facilities and
32   report annually on this review to the legislature.
33        (2) The tolling authority, in determining toll rates, shall
34   consider the policy guidelines established in RCW 47.56.830.
35        (3) Unless otherwise directed by the legislature, in setting and
36   periodically adjusting toll rates, the tolling authority must ensure
37   that toll rates will generate revenue sufficient to:



                                            p. 49                          ESSB 5801.SL
 1        (a) Meet the operating costs of the eligible toll facilities,
 2   including necessary maintenance, preservation, renewal, replacement,
 3   administration, and toll enforcement by public law enforcement;
 4        (b) Meet obligations for the timely payment of debt service on
 5   bonds issued for eligible toll facilities, and any other associated
 6   financing costs including, but not limited to, required reserves,
 7   minimum debt coverage or other appropriate contingency funding,
 8   insurance,  and  compliance  with  all  other  financial  and  other
 9   covenants made by the state in the bond proceedings;
10        (c) Meet obligations to reimburse the motor vehicle fund for
11   excise taxes on motor vehicle and special fuels applied to the
12   payment of bonds issued for eligible toll facilities; and
13        (d) Meet any other obligations of the tolling authority to
14   provide its proportionate share of funding contributions for any
15   projects or operations of the eligible toll facilities.
16        (4) The established toll rates may include variable pricing, and
17   should be set to optimize system performance, recognizing necessary
18   trade-offs  to  generate  revenue  for  the  purposes  specified  in
19   subsection    (3) of this section. Tolls may vary for type of vehicle,
20   time of day, traffic conditions, or other factors designed to improve
21   performance of the system.
22        (5) In fixing and adjusting toll rates under this section, the
23   only toll revenue to be taken into account must be toll revenue
24   pledged to bonds that includes toll receipts, and the only debt
25   service requirements to be taken into account must be debt service on
26   bonds payable from and secured by toll revenue that includes toll
27   receipts.
28        (6) The legislature pledges to appropriate toll revenue as
29   necessary to carry out the purposes of this section. When the
30   legislature has specifically identified and designated an eligible
31   toll facility and authorized the issuance of bonds for the financing
32   of the eligible toll facility that are payable from and secured by a
33   pledge of toll revenue, the legislature further agrees for the
34   benefit of the owners of outstanding bonds issued by the state for
35   eligible toll facilities to continue in effect and not to impair or
36   withdraw the authorization of the tolling authority to fix and adjust
37   tolls as provided in this section. The state finance committee shall
38   pledge the state's obligation to impose and maintain tolls, together
39   with the application of toll revenue as described in this section, to
40   the owners of any bonds.

13   partial destruction. Repair of a structure or development may not

18        (5)(a) No local comprehensive plan or development regulation may

20        (b) A city or county precludes an essential public facility when
21   the city or county imposes conditions or costs that the city or
22   county  cannot  demonstrate  are  reasonably  necessary  to  mitigate
23   adverse impacts directly caused by construction or operation of the
24   essential public facility. A city or county with permitting authority
25   shall commit to reasonable timelines to ensure timely issuance of
26   permits without unnecessary delay. The essential public facility
27   shall provide the city or county with the information needed to make
28   timely  permitting  decisions.  This  subsection             (5)(b)    is  limited
29   exclusively  to  those  essential  public  facilities  that  are
30   improvements to high capacity transportation systems as defined in
31   RCW 81.104.015.
32        (6) No person may bring a cause of action for civil damages based
33   on the good faith actions of any county or city to provide for the
34   siting of secure community transition facilities in accordance with
35   this section and with the requirements of chapter 12, Laws of 2001
36   2nd sp. sess. For purposes of this subsection, "person" includes, but
37   is  not  limited  to,  any  individual,  agency  as  defined  in  RCW
38   42.17A.005,  corporation,  partnership,  association,  and  limited
39   liability entity.


                                            p. 59                          ESSB 5801.SL
 1        (7) Counties or cities siting facilities pursuant to subsection
 2   (2) or (3) of this section shall comply with RCW 71.09.341.
 3        (8) The failure of a county or city to act by the deadlines
 4   established in subsections (2) and (3) of this section is not:
 5        (a) A condition that would disqualify the county or city for
 6   grants, loans, or pledges under RCW 43.155.070 or 70A.135.070;
 7        (b) A consideration for grants or loans provided under RCW
 8   43.17.250(3); or
 9        (c) A basis for any petition under RCW             36.70A.280 or for any
10   private cause of action.


11        Sec. 604.     RCW 36.70A.200 and 2024 c 164 s 511 are each amended
12   to read as follows:
13        (1)(a) The comprehensive plan of each county and city that is
14   planning under RCW 36.70A.040 shall include a process for identifying
15   and siting essential public facilities. Essential public facilities
16   include those facilities that are typically difficult to site, such
17   as  airports,  state  education  facilities  and  state  or  regional
18   transportation facilities as defined in RCW                47.06.140, regional

27        NEW SECTION.  Sec. 605.  A new section is added to chapter 43.21C
28   RCW to read as follows:
29        In the event of a disagreement over the scope of a transit
30   project, state agencies, cities, and counties shall accept the
31   detailed  statement  prepared  by  the  transit  agency  under  RCW
32   43.21C.030(2)(c) as the sole environmental review document, rather
33   than  conducting  separate  environmental  reviews  or  preparing
34   additional detailed statements. Consistent with RCW             43.21C.150, when
35   a  transit  agency  has  previously  prepared  an  adequate  detailed
36   statement pursuant to the national environmental policy act of 1969
37   as  part  of  a  federally  funded  transit  project,  that  national
38   environmental policy act document shall satisfy the requirements
39   under RCW     43.21C.030(2)(c). State agencies, cities, and counties
                                            p. 62                         ESSB 5801.SL
 1   shall adopt and rely on the national environmental policy act
 2   document for their environmental review and permitting processes,
 3   aligning applicable local documents accordingly. For purposes of this
 4   section,  "transit  agency"  does  not  include  a  regional  transit
 5   authority under chapter 81.112 RCW.


 6                      PART VII: TRANSPORTATION GRANT PROGRAMS


 7        NEW SECTION.       Sec. 701.      A new county local road program is
 8   established to fund the preservation and improvement of county local
 9   roads. The board must:
10        (1) Adopt rules necessary to implement the provisions of this
11   chapter relating to the allocation of funds; and
12        (2) Include a program status report in the board's annual report
13   to the legislature as provided in RCW 36.78.070.


14        NEW SECTION.      Sec. 702.     The definitions in this section apply
15   throughout  this  chapter  unless  the  context  clearly  requires
16   otherwise.
17        (1) "Board" means the county road administration board created in
18   RCW 36.78.030.
19        (2) "Community facility" means a publicly owned facility or
20   building that is primarily intended to serve the recreational,
21   educational, cultural, public health and safety, administrative, or
22   entertainment needs of the community as a whole.
23        (3)  "County  local  road  program  project"  means  improvement
24   projects on those county roads not federally classified as an
25   arterial or collector.
26        (4)  "LAG  manual"  means  the  Washington  state  department  of
27   transportation's local agency guidelines manual or its successor
28   document.
29        (5) "Overburdened community" has the same meaning as defined in
30   RCW 70A.02.010.
31        (6) "Pedestrian facility" means a facility designed to meet the
32   needs of pedestrians in accordance with county and Americans with
33   disabilities act requirements.


34        NEW SECTION.       Sec. 703.      (1) The board shall adopt rules to
35   select preservation and improvement projects under this chapter


                                            p. 63                         ESSB 5801.SL
 1   taking into consideration, at a minimum, the following priority
 2   rating factors:
 3        (a) Investment in overburdened communities;
 4        (b)  Environmental  health  disparities  as  identified  in  the
 5   environmental health disparities map specified in RCW 43.70.815;
 6        (c) Location on or providing direct access to a federally
 7   recognized Indian reservation or lands;
 8        (d) Sustaining the structural, safety, and operational integrity
 9   of the road;
10        (e) Vehicle and pedestrian collision experience;
11        (f) Access improvements to a community facility; and
12        (g) Identified need in a state, regional, county, or community
13   plan.
14        (2) Proposed projects must be included in the respective county's
15   six-year plan as provided in RCW          36.81.121 before board approval of
16   the project.


17        NEW SECTION.  Sec. 704.  The following project types are allowed
18   under the county local road program created in this chapter:
19        (1) 2-R as defined in the LAG manual;
20        (2) 3-R as defined in the LAG manual;
21        (3) Reconstruction as defined in the LAG manual;
22        (4) Replacement of any bridge on the national bridge inventory;
23        (5) Removal of human-made or caused impediments to anadromous
24   fish passage; and
25        (6) Pedestrian facilities.


26        NEW SECTION.      Sec. 705.      Whenever a proposed county local road
27   program project is adjacent to a city or town, the appropriate city
28   or town and county officials shall jointly plan and include the
29   improvement in their respective long-range plans. Whenever a county
30   local road program project connects with and will be substantially
31   affected by a programmed construction project on a state highway, the
32   proper county officials shall jointly plan the development of such
33   project with the department of transportation district administrator.


34        NEW SECTION.  Sec. 706.  Counties receiving funds from the county
35   local road program shall provide such matching funds as established
36   by  rules  adopted  by  the  board.  Matching  requirements  must  be


                                             p. 64                         ESSB 5801.SL
 1   established after appropriate studies by the board and considering
 2   the financial resources available to counties.


 3        NEW SECTION.  Sec. 707.  (1) Only those counties that, during the
 4   preceding 12 months, have spent all revenues collected for road
 5   purposes only for such purposes, including removal of barriers to
 6   fish passage and accompanying streambed and stream bank repair as
 7   specified in RCW     36.82.070, and including traffic law enforcement as
 8   allowed under Article II, section 40 of the state Constitution or RCW
 9   36.82.070(2), are eligible to receive funds from the county local
10   road program, except that:
11        (a) Counties with a population of less than 8,000 are exempt from
12   this eligibility restriction;
13        (b) Counties expending revenues collected for road purposes only
14   on other governmental services after authorization from the voters of
15   that county under RCW         84.55.050 are exempt from this eligibility
16   restriction; and
17        (c) This restriction does not apply to any moneys diverted from
18   the road district levy under chapter 39.89 RCW.
19        (2) The board shall authorize county local road grant program
20   funds for the construction project portion of a project previously
21   authorized for a preliminary proposal in the sequence in which the
22   preliminary proposal has been completed and the construction project
23   is to be placed under contract. At such time the board may reserve
24   funds for expenditure in future years as may be necessary for
25   completion of preliminary proposals and construction projects to be
26   commenced in the ensuing biennium.
27        (3) Subject to the availability of amounts appropriated for this
28   specific purpose, the board may consider additional projects for
29   authorization under this chapter upon a clear and conclusive showing
30   by the submitting county that the proposed project is of an emergent
31   nature and that its need was unable to be anticipated at the time the
32   six-year plan of the county was developed. The proposed projects must
33   be evaluated on the basis of the priority rating factors specified in
34   section 703 of this act.


35        NEW SECTION.      Sec. 708.      Whenever the board approves a county
36   local road program project under this chapter it shall determine the
37   amount of county local road program funds to be allocated for such
38   project. The allocation must be based upon information submitted by
                                            p. 65                          ESSB 5801.SL
 1   the county seeking approval of the project and upon such further
 2   investigation as the board deems necessary. The board shall adopt
 3   reasonable rules pursuant to which county local road program funds
 4   allocated to a project may be increased upon a subsequent application
 5   of the county constructing the project. The rules adopted by the
 6   board must take into account, but are not limited to, the following
 7   factors:
 8        (1) The financial effect of increasing the original allocation
 9   for the project upon other county local road program projects either
10   approved or requested;
11        (2) Whether the project for which an additional allocation is
12   requested can be reduced in scope while retaining a usable segment;
13        (3) Whether the original cost of the project shown in the
14   applicant's original submittal was based upon reasonable engineering
15   estimates; and
16        (4) Whether the requested additional allocation is to pay for an
17   expansion in the scope of work originally approved.


18        NEW SECTION.      Sec. 709.      Sections 701 through 708 of this act
19   constitute a new chapter in Title 36 RCW.


20        Sec. 710.  RCW 47.04.380 and 2024 c 106 s 1 are each amended to
21   read as follows:
22        (1) The legislature finds that many communities across Washington
23   state have not equitably benefited from investments in the active
24   transportation network. The legislature also finds that legacy state
25   transportation facilities designed primarily for vehicle use caused
26   disconnections in safe routes for people who walk, bike, and roll to
27   work and to carry out other daily activities.
28        (2) To address these investment gaps, and to honor the legacy of
29   community advocacy of Sandy Williams, the Sandy Williams connecting
30   communities program is established within the department. The purpose
31   of the program is to improve active transportation connectivity in
32   communities by:
33        (a)  Providing  safe,  continuous  routes  for  pedestrians,
34   bicyclists, and other nonvehicle users carrying out their daily
35   activities;
36        (b) Mitigating for the health, safety, and access impacts of
37   transportation infrastructure that bisects communities and creates
38   obstacles in the local active transportation network;
                                            p. 66                         ESSB 5801.SL
 1        (c) Investing in greenways providing protected routes for a wide
 2   variety of nonvehicular users; and
 3        (d) Facilitating the planning, development, and implementation of
 4   projects and activities that will improve the connectivity and safety
 5   of the active transportation network.
 6        (3) The department must select projects to propose to the
 7   legislature for funding. In selecting projects, the department must
 8   consider, at a minimum, the following criteria:
 9        (a) Access to a transit facility, community facility, commercial
10   center, or community-identified assets;
11        (b) The use of minority and women-owned businesses and community-
12   based organizations in planning, community engagement, design, and
13   construction of the project;
14        (c) Whether the project will serve:
15        (i) Overburdened communities as defined in RCW 70A.02.010 to mean
16   a  geographic  area  where  vulnerable  populations  face  combined,
17   multiple environmental harms and health impacts, and includes, but is
18   not limited to, highly impacted communities as defined in RCW
19   19.405.020;
20        (ii) Vulnerable populations as defined in RCW 70A.02.010 to mean
21   population groups that are more likely to be at higher risk for poor
22   health outcomes in response to environmental harms, due to adverse
23   socioeconomic  factors,  such  as  unemployment,  high  housing,  and
24   transportation costs relative to income, limited access to nutritious
25   food and adequate health care, linguistic isolation, and other
26   factors  that  negatively  affect  health  outcomes  and  increase
27   vulnerability to the effects of environmental harms; and sensitivity
28   factors,  such  as  low  birth  weight  and  higher  rates  of
29   hospitalization. Vulnerable populations include, but are not limited
30   to: Racial or ethnic minorities, low-income populations, populations
31   disproportionately impacted by environmental harms, and populations
32   of workers experiencing environmental harms;
33        (iii) Household incomes at or below 200 percent of the federal
34   poverty level; and
35        (iv) People with disabilities;
36        (d) Environmental health disparities, such as those indicated by
37   the diesel pollution burden portion of the Washington environmental
38   health disparities map developed by the department of health, or
39   other similar indicators;


                                            p. 67                          ESSB 5801.SL
 1        (e)  Location  on  or  adjacent  to  tribal  lands  or  locations
 2   providing essential services to tribal members;
 3        (f) Crash experience involving pedestrians and bicyclists; and
 4        (g) Identified need by the community, for example in the state
 5   active transportation plan or a regional, county, or community plan.
 6        (4) It is the intent of the legislature that the Sandy Williams
 7   connecting  communities  program  comply  with  the  requirements  of
 8   chapter 314, Laws of 2021.
 9        (5) The department shall submit a report to the transportation
10   committees of the legislature by December 1, 2022, and each December
11   1st  thereafter  identifying  the  selected  connecting  communities
12   projects for funding by the legislature. The report must also include
13   the status of previously funded projects.
14        (6) The Sandy Williams connecting communities program account is
15   created in the state treasury. Moneys in the account may be spent
16   only after appropriation. Expenditures from the account may be used
17   only for the program activities described in this section.
18        (7)   Beginning September 2027, by the last day of September,
19   December, March, and June of each year, the state treasurer shall
20   transfer  $3,125,000  from  the  multimodal  transportation  account
21   created in RCW 47.66.070 to the Sandy Williams connecting communities
22   program account created in this section.

40        (d) People with disabilities;
                                            p. 69                          ESSB 5801.SL

32   account is subject in all respects to              chapter    43.88 RCW, but no

36   improvement act fall under RCW             43.88.180 and shall not require
                                            p. 70                          ESSB 5801.SL
 1   appropriation. The office of financial management shall determine the
 2   amounts due to or from the federal government pursuant to the cash
 3   management improvement act. The office of financial management may
 4   direct transfers of funds between accounts as deemed necessary to
 5   implement the provisions of the cash management improvement act, and
 6   this subsection. Refunds or allocations shall occur prior to the
 7   distributions of earnings set forth in subsection                   (4) of this
 8   section.
 9        (3) Except for the provisions of RCW             43.84.160, the treasury
10   income account may be utilized for the payment of purchased banking
11   services on behalf of treasury funds including, but not limited to,

13   treasury and affected state agencies. The treasury income account is

17   section.

19        Sec. 804.  RCW 70A.65.030 and 2023 c 475 s 1902 and 2023 c 475 s
20   936 are each reenacted and amended to read as follows:
21        (1)   ((Except as provided in subsection            (4)   of this section,
22   each))   Each year or biennium, as appropriate, when allocating funds
23   from  the  carbon  emissions  reduction  account  created  in  RCW
24   70A.65.240, the climate commitment account created in RCW 70A.65.260,
25   the natural climate solutions account created in RCW 70A.65.270, the

27   quality and health disparities improvement account created in RCW

29   46.68.500, or the climate active transportation account created in
30   RCW   46.68.490,)) or administering grants or programs funded by the
31   accounts, agencies shall conduct an environmental justice assessment
32   consistent with the requirements of RCW            70A.02.060 and establish a
33   minimum of not less than 35 percent and a goal of 40 percent of total
34   investments that provide direct and meaningful benefits to vulnerable
35   populations  within  the  boundaries  of  overburdened  communities
36   through:    (a)  The  direct  reduction  of  environmental  burdens  in
37   overburdened communities;         (b) the reduction of disproportionate,
38   cumulative  risk  from  environmental  burdens,  including  those
39   associated with climate change;           (c) the support of community led
                                            p. 78                         ESSB 5801.SL
 1   project  development,  planning,  and  participation  costs;  or                 (d)
 2   meeting  a  community  need  identified  by  the  community  that  is
 3   consistent with the intent of this chapter or RCW 70A.02.010.
 4        (2) The allocation of funding under subsection                  (1) of this
 5   section must adhere to the following principles, additional to the
 6   requirements of RCW      70A.02.080:    (a) Benefits and programs should be
 7   directed  to  areas  and  targeted  to  vulnerable  populations  and
 8   overburdened  communities  to  reduce  statewide  disparities;                   (b)
 9   investments and benefits should be made roughly proportional to the
10   health disparities that a specific community experiences, with a goal
11   of eliminating the disparities;         (c) investments and programs should
12   focus  on  creating  environmental  benefits,  including  eliminating
13   health burdens, creating community and population resilience, and
14   raising the quality of life of those in the community; and                       (d)
15   efforts should be made to balance investments and benefits across the
16   state and within counties, local jurisdictions, and unincorporated
17   areas as appropriate to reduce disparities by location and to ensure
18   efforts contribute to a reduction in disparities that exist based on
19   race or ethnicity, socioeconomic status, or other factors.

21   state))   State agencies allocating funds or administering grants or

30        (a) Report annually to the environmental justice council created

32   justice and environmental health goals;

39   administering grants or programs from the climate investment account.

36        Sec. 806.  RCW 70A.65.230 and 2022 c 182 s 426 and 2022 c 181 s 8
37   are each reenacted and amended to read as follows:
38        (1) It is the intent of the legislature that each year the total
39   investments made through the carbon emissions reduction account
                                            p. 81                         ESSB 5801.SL
 1   created in RCW 70A.65.240, the climate commitment account created in
 2   RCW 70A.65.260, the natural climate solutions account created in RCW
 3   70A.65.270,    and the air quality and health disparities improvement
 4   account created in RCW         70A.65.280,    ((the climate transit programs
 5   account  created  in  RCW           46.68.500,  and  the  climate  active
 6   transportation  account  created  in  RCW           46.68.490,))  achieve  the
 7   following:
 8        (a) A minimum of not less than 35 percent and a goal of 40
 9   percent of total investments that provide direct and meaningful
10   benefits  to  vulnerable  populations  within  the  boundaries  of
11   overburdened communities identified under chapter 70A.02 RCW; and
12        (b) In addition to the requirements of (a) of this subsection, a
13   minimum of not less than 10 percent of total investments that are
14   used for programs, activities, or projects formally supported by a
15   resolution of an Indian tribe, with priority given to otherwise
16   qualifying projects directly administered or proposed by an Indian
17   tribe. An investment that meets the requirements of both this
18   subsection    (1)(b) and    (a) of this subsection may count toward the
19   minimum percentage targets for both subsections.
20        (2)  The  expenditure  of  moneys  under  this  chapter  must  be
21   consistent with applicable federal, state, and local laws, and treaty
22   rights including, but not limited to, prohibitions on uses of funds
23   imposed by the state Constitution.
24        (3)  For  the  purposes  of  this  section,  "benefits"  means
25   investments or activities that:
26        (a) Reduce vulnerable population characteristics, environmental
27   burdens, or associated risks that contribute significantly to the
28   cumulative impact designation of overburdened communities;
29        (b) Meaningfully protect an overburdened community from, or
30   support community response to, the impacts of air pollution or
31   climate change; or
32        (c) Meet a community need identified by vulnerable members of the
33   overburdened community that is consistent with the intent of this
34   chapter.
35        (4) The state must develop a process by which to evaluate the
36   impacts of the investments made under this chapter, work across state
37   agencies  to  develop  and  track  priorities  across  the  different
38   eligible funding categories, and work with the environmental justice
39   council pursuant to RCW 70A.65.040.

 1        NEW SECTION.  Sec. 807.  Any residual balance of funds remaining
 2   in the climate transit programs account or the climate active
 3   transportation account on June 30, 2025, shall be transferred by the
 4   state treasurer to the carbon emissions reduction account.


 5                       Zero Emission Vehicle Tax Incentives


 6        NEW SECTION.       Sec. 808.      This section is the tax preference
 7   performance statement for the tax preferences contained in sections
 8   809 and 810, chapter . . ., Laws of 2025 (sections 809 and 810 of
 9   this act). This performance statement is only intended to be used for
10   subsequent evaluation of the tax preference. It is not intended to
11   create a private right of action by any party or be used to determine
12   eligibility for preferential tax treatment.
13        (1) The legislature categorizes the tax preferences as ones
14   intended to induce certain designated behavior by taxpayers, as
15   indicated in RCW 82.32.808(2)(a).
16        (2) It is the legislature's specific public policy objective to
17   increase the use of zero emission buses by transit agencies in
18   Washington.  It  is  the  legislature's  intent  to  extend  the  tax
19   incentive available to zero emission buses to further emission
20   reductions, as well as reductions in fine particulates, in the state.
21        (3) To measure the effectiveness of the tax preferences in
22   sections 809 and 810, chapter . . ., Laws of 2025 (sections 809 and
23   810 of this act) in achieving the public policy objectives described
24   in subsection     (2) of this section, the joint legislative audit and
25   review committee must evaluate the number of zero emission transit
26   buses titled in the state and the estimated resulting carbon emission
27   and fine particulate reductions.
28        (4) In order to obtain the data necessary to perform the review
29   in subsection      (3) of this section, the department of licensing,
30   department of revenue, and department of ecology must provide data
31   needed for the joint legislative audit and review committee analysis.
32   In addition to the data source described under this subsection, the
33   joint legislative audit and review committee may use any other data
34   it deems necessary.


35        NEW SECTION.  Sec. 809.  A new section is added to chapter 82.08
36   RCW to read as follows:


                                            p. 83                         ESSB 5801.SL
 1        (1) The tax levied by RCW        82.08.020 does not apply to sales of
 2   zero emission buses purchased by:
 3        (a) A transit agency; or
 4        (b)  A  federally  recognized  Indian  tribe  to  provide  public
 5   transportation services.
 6        (2) Sellers may make tax exempt sales under this section only if
 7   the buyer provides the seller with an exemption certificate in a form
 8   and manner prescribed by the department. The seller must retain a
 9   copy of the certificate for the seller's files.
10        (3) For the purposes of this section:
11        (a) "Transit agency" means a city-owned transit system, county
12   transportation authority, metropolitan municipal corporation, public
13   transportation benefit area, unincorporated transportation benefit
14   area, or regional transit authority.
15        (b) "Zero emission bus" means a bus that emits no exhaust gas
16   from the onboard source of power, other than water vapor.
17        (4) On the last day of February, May, August, and November of
18   each year, the state treasurer, based upon information provided by
19   the department, must transfer from the carbon emissions reduction
20   account to the general fund a sum equal to the dollar amount that
21   would otherwise have been deposited into the general fund during the
22   prior calendar quarter but for the exemption provided in this section
23   and section 810 of this act. Information provided by the department
24   to the state treasurer must be based on the best available data,
25   except that the department may provide estimates of taxes exempted
26   under this section until such time as retailers are able to report
27   such exempted amounts on their tax returns.
28        (5)(a) The department must provide notification on its website
29   monthly of the amount in exemptions issued and the amount remaining
30   under this section and section 810 of this act before the limit
31   described in (b) of this subsection has been reached, and, once that
32   limit has been reached, the date the exemption expires pursuant to
33   (b) of this subsection.
34        (b) The exemption under this section expires after the last day
35   of the calendar month immediately following the month the department
36   determines the total amount of state sales and use tax exemptions
37   issued under this section and section 810 of this act reaches or
38   exceeds $14,000,000.

                                            p. 84                          ESSB 5801.SL

 7   exempted under this section and section 810 of this act, by fiscal
 8   year.

11        Sec. 812.  RCW 47.04.350 and 2019 c 287 s 3 are each amended to
12   read as follows:
13        (1)   ((Subject to the availability of amounts appropriated for
14   this specific purpose through the 2023-2025 biennium, the))                     The
15   department's  public-private  partnership  office  must  develop  and
16   maintain a program to support the deployment of clean alternative
17   fuel vehicle charging and refueling infrastructure that is supported
18   by private financing.
19        (2) The department must define corridors in which bidders may
20   propose  to  install  electric  vehicle  charging  infrastructure  or
21   hydrogen fueling stations, and may update these corridors over time
22   as needed. Alternatively, a bidder may propose a corridor in which
23   the bidder proposes to install electric vehicle infrastructure or
24   hydrogen  fueling  stations  if  the  department  has  adopted  rules
25   allowing such a proposal and establishing guidelines for how such a
26   proposal will be considered.
27        (3)(a) For bid proposals under this section, the department must
28   require the following:
29        (i) Bidders must have private sector partners contributing to the
30   project who stand to gain indirect value from development of the
31   project, such as motor vehicle manufacturers, retail stores, or
32   tourism stakeholders;
33        (ii) Bidders must demonstrate that the proposed project will be
34   valuable to clean alternative fuel vehicle drivers and will address
35   an  existing  gap  in  the  state's  low  carbon  transportation
36   infrastructure;
37        (iii) Projects must be expected to be profitable and sustainable
38   for the owner-operator and the private partner; and


                                            p. 86                          ESSB 5801.SL
 1        (iv) Bidders must specify how the project captures the indirect
 2   value of charging or refueling station deployment to the private
 3   partner.
 4        (b) The department may adopt rules that require any other
 5   criteria for a successful project.
 6        (4) In evaluating proposals under this section, the department
 7   may use the electric vehicle financial analysis tool that was
 8   developed  in  the  joint  transportation  committee's  study  into
 9   financing electric vehicle charging station infrastructure.
10        (5)(a) After selecting a successful proposer under this section,
11   the department may provide a loan or grant to the proposer.
12        (b)   ((Grants and loans issued under this subsection must be
13   funded from the electric vehicle account created in RCW 82.44.200.
14        (c))) Any project selected for support under this section is
15   eligible for only one grant or loan as a part of the program.
16        (6)  The  department  may  conduct  preliminary  workshops  with
17   potential bidders and other potential private sector partners to
18   determine the best method of designing and maintaining the program,
19   discuss how to develop and maintain the partnerships among the
20   private sector partners that may receive indirect value, and any
21   other issues relating to the implementation and administration of
22   this section. The department should consider regional workshops to
23   engage potential business partners from across the state.
24        (7) The department must adopt rules to implement and administer
25   this section.


26        Sec. 813.  RCW 47.04.355 and 2019 c 287 s 16 are each amended to
27   read as follows:
28        (1)  ((Subject to the availability of amounts appropriated for

                                           p. 87                          ESSB 5801.SL
 1   shared mobility pilot programs are eligible to participate in this

26   infrastructure, staff time, and any other expenses required to
27   implement the project. No more than ((ten)) 10 percent of grant funds

31   program purposes and, if sold, the proceeds of the sale must be used
32   solely for program purposes.

34   fuel car sharing services, the state must be reimbursed for any
35   property acquired with state grant funding under this section that
36   nongovernmental participants in the program retain at the time of
37   program  termination.  The  amount  of  reimbursement  may  under  no
38   circumstances be less than the fair market value of the property at
