 1                         Fuel Conversion Activity Reporting


 2        NEW SECTION.  Sec. 814.  A new section is added to chapter 70A.65
 3   RCW to read as follows:
 4        (1) State agencies that receive or have received appropriations
 5   from  the  carbon  emissions  reduction  account  in  an  omnibus
 6   transportation appropriations act are required to report information
 7   to estimate emission reductions from fuel conversion activities
 8   funded from these appropriations to the legislature, as well as any
 9   requested information necessary for the estimation and analysis of
10   projected and realized emission reductions, using the reporting tool
11   developed by the joint transportation committee in accordance with
12   section 204(7), chapter 472, Laws of 2023 in a form and manner
13   prescribed by the joint transportation committee.
14        (2) Reports must include initial reporting of projected emission
15   reductions at the time of expenditure and continued reporting of
16   factors  to  be  used  to  calculate  estimated  realized  emission
17   reductions in subsequent years.
18        (3) The reporting requirement in this section is in addition to
19   the reporting requirements of RCW 70A.65.300.
20        (4) For purposes of this section, "fuel conversion" means the
21   purchase of zero emission or hybrid electric vehicles, vessels, or
22   off-road equipment and the charging or fueling infrastructure needed
23   to support zero emission or hybrid electric vehicles or vessels.


24      PART IX: TRAFFIC SAFETY, ACTIVE TRANSPORTATION, AND RELATED POLICY


25                                   Complete Streets


26        Sec. 901.  RCW 47.04.035 and 2022 c 182 s 418 are each amended to
27   read as follows:
28        (1) In order to improve the safety, mobility, and accessibility
29   of state highways, it is the intent of the legislature that the
30   department must incorporate the principles of complete streets with
31   facilities that provide street access with all users in mind,
32   including pedestrians, bicyclists, and public transportation users,
33   notwithstanding  the  provisions  of  RCW                 47.24.020  concerning
34   responsibility beyond the curb of state rights-of-way. As such, state
35   transportation projects       (a) starting design      ((on or after))      between
36   July 1, 2022, and      July 31, 2025, that are $500,000 or more,           and (b)
                                            p. 89                          ESSB 5801.SL
 1   starting design on or after August 1, 2025, that are $1,000,000 or
 2   more, must:
 3        (((a)))   (i) Identify those locations on state rights-of-way that
 4   do not have a complete and Americans with disabilities act accessible
 5   sidewalk or shared-use path, that do not have bicycle facilities in
 6   the form of a bike lane or adjacent parallel trail or shared-use
 7   path, that have such facilities on a state route within a population
 8   center that has a posted speed in excess of 30 miles per hour and no
 9   buffer or physical separation from vehicular traffic for pedestrians
10   and bicyclists, and/or that have a design that hampers the ability of
11   motorists to see a crossing pedestrian with sufficient time to stop
12   given posted speed limits and roadway configuration;
13        (((b))) (ii) Consult with local jurisdictions to confirm existing
14   and planned active transportation connections along or across the
15   location; identification of connections to existing and planned
16   public  transportation  services,  ferry  landings,  commuter  and
17   passenger rail, and airports; the existing and planned facility
18   type(s) within the local jurisdiction that connect to the location;
19   and the potential use of speed management techniques to minimize
20   crash exposure and severity;
21        (((c)))   (iii) Adjust the speed limit to a lower speed with
22   appropriate modifications to roadway design and operations to achieve
23   the desired operating speed in those locations where this speed
24   management  approach  aligns  with  local  plans  or  ordinances,
25   particularly in those contexts that present a higher possibility of
26   serious injury or fatal crashes occurring based on land use context,
27   observed crash data, crash potential, roadway characteristics that
28   are likely to increase exposure, or a combination thereof, in keeping
29   with a safe system approach and with the intention of ultimately
30   eliminating serious and fatal crashes; and
31        (((d)))   (iv) Plan, design, and construct facilities providing
32   context-sensitive solutions that contribute to network connectivity
33   and safety for pedestrians, bicyclists, and people accessing public
34   transportation  and  other  modal  connections,  such  facilities  to
35   include Americans with disabilities act accessible sidewalks or
36   shared-use paths, bicyclist facilities, and crossings as needed to
37   integrate the state route into the local network.
38        (2) Projects undertaken for emergent work required to reopen a
39   state highway in the event of a natural disaster or other emergency


                                            p. 90                         ESSB 5801.SL
 1   repair are not required to comply with the provisions of this
 2   section.
 3        (3) Maintenance of facilities constructed under this provision
 4   shall be as provided under existing law.
 5        (4) This section does not create a private right of action.


 6                     Traffic Safety and Tribal Representation


 7        Sec. 902.      RCW 43.59.156 and 2020 c 72 s 1 are each amended to
 8   read as follows:
 9        (1) Within amounts appropriated to the traffic safety commission,
10   the commission must convene the Cooper Jones active transportation
11   safety council comprised of stakeholders who have a unique interest
12   or expertise in the safety of pedestrians, bicyclists, and other
13   nonmotorists.
14        (2) The purpose of the council is to review and analyze data and
15   programs  related  to  fatalities  and  serious  injuries  involving
16   pedestrians, bicyclists, and other nonmotorists to identify points at
17   which the transportation system can be improved including, whenever
18   possible, privately owned areas of the system such as parking lots,
19   and  to  identify  patterns  in  pedestrian,  bicyclist,  and  other
20   nonmotorist fatalities and serious injuries. The council may also:
21        (a)  Monitor  progress  on  implementation  of  existing  council
22   recommendations; and
23        (b) Seek opportunities to expand consideration and implementation
24   of the principles of systematic safety, including areas where data
25   collection may need improvement.
26        (3)(a) The council may include, but is not limited to:
27        (i) A representative from the commission;
28        (ii) A coroner from the county in which pedestrian, bicyclist, or
29   nonmotorist deaths have occurred;
30        (iii) Multiple members of law enforcement who have investigated
31   pedestrian, bicyclist, or nonmotorist fatalities;
32        (iv) A traffic engineer;
33        (v) A representative from the department of transportation and a
34   representative from the department of health;
35        (vi) A representative from the association of Washington cities;
36        (vii) A representative from the Washington state association of
37   counties;
38        (viii) A representative from a pedestrian advocacy group; ((and))
                                             p. 91                          ESSB 5801.SL
 1        (ix) A representative from a tribal government; and
 2        (x) A representative from a bicyclist or other nonmotorist
 3   advocacy group.
 4        (b)  The  commission  may  invite  other  representatives  of
 5   stakeholder  groups  to  participate  in  the  council  as  deemed
 6   appropriate by the commission. Additionally, the commission may
 7   invite a victim or family member of a victim to participate in the
 8   council.
 9        (4) The council must meet at least quarterly. By December 31st of
10   each year, the council must issue an annual report detailing any
11   findings and recommendations to the governor and the transportation
12   committees of the legislature. The commission must provide the annual
13   report electronically to all municipal governments and state agencies
14   that  participated  in  the  council  during  that  calendar  year.
15   Additionally,  the  council  must  report  any  budgetary  or  fiscal
16   recommendations  to  the  office  of  financial  management  and  the
17   legislature by August 1st on a biennial basis.
18        (5)  As  part  of  the  review  of  pedestrian,  bicyclist,  or
19   nonmotorist fatalities and serious injuries that occur in Washington,
20   the council may review any available information, including crash
21   information  maintained  in  existing  databases;  statutes,  rules,
22   policies, or ordinances governing pedestrians and traffic related to
23   the incidents; and any other relevant information. The council may
24   make  recommendations  regarding  changes  in  statutes,  ordinances,
25   rules, and policies that could improve pedestrian, bicyclist, or
26   nonmotorist   safety.   Additionally,   the   council   may   make
27   recommendations on how to improve traffic fatality and serious injury
28   data  quality,  including  crashes  that  occur  in  privately  owned
29   property such as parking lots. The council may consult with local
30   cities and counties, as well as local police departments and other
31   law  enforcement  agencies  and  associations  representing  those
32   jurisdictions on how to improve data quality regarding crashes
33   occurring on private property.
34        (6)(a) Documents prepared by or for the council are inadmissible
35   and may not be used in a civil or administrative proceeding, except
36   that any document that exists before its use or consideration in a
37   review by the council, or that is created independently of such
38   review, does not become inadmissible merely because it is reviewed or
39   used by the council. For confidential information, such as personally
40   identifiable information and medical records, which are obtained by
                                            p. 92                          ESSB 5801.SL
 1   the council, neither the commission nor the council may publicly
 2   disclose  such  confidential  information.  No  person  who  was  in
 3   attendance at a meeting of the council or who participated in the
 4   creation, retention, collection, or maintenance of information or
 5   documents specifically for the commission or the council shall be
 6   permitted to testify in any civil action as to the content of such
 7   proceedings or of the documents and information prepared specifically
 8   as part of the activities of the council. However, recommendations
 9   from the council and the commission generally may be disclosed
10   without personal identifiers.
11        (b)  The  council  may  review,  only  to  the  extent  otherwise
12   permitted by law or court rule when determined to be relevant and
13   necessary:  Any  law  enforcement  incident  documentation,  such  as
14   incident reports, dispatch records, and victim, witness, and suspect
15   statements; any supplemental reports, probable cause statements, and
16   911 call taker's reports; and any other information determined to be
17   relevant to the review. The commission and the council must maintain
18   the confidentiality of such information to the extent required by any
19   applicable law.
20        (7) If acting in good faith, without malice, and within the
21   parameters  of  and  protocols  established  under  this  chapter,
22   representatives of the commission and the council are immune from
23   civil liability for an activity related to reviews of particular
24   fatalities and serious injuries.
25        (8) This section must not be construed to provide a private civil
26   cause of action.
27        (9)(a) The council may receive gifts, grants, or endowments from
28   public or private sources that are made from time to time, in trust
29   or otherwise, for the use and benefit of the purposes of the council
30   and spend the gifts, grants, or endowments from the public or private
31   sources according to their terms, unless the receipt of the gifts,
32   grants, or endowments violates RCW 42.17A.560.
33        (b) Subject to the appropriation of funds for this specific
34   purpose,  the  council  may  provide  grants  targeted  at  improving
35   pedestrian, bicyclist, or nonmotorist safety in accordance with
36   recommendations made by the council.
37        (10) For purposes of this section:
38        (a) "Bicyclist fatality" means any death of a bicyclist resulting
39   from a collision, whether on a roadway, at an intersection, along an
40   adjacent sidewalk, or on a path that is contiguous with a roadway.
                                            p. 93                          ESSB 5801.SL
 1        (b) "Council" means the Cooper Jones active transportation safety
 2   council.
 3        (c) "Nonmotorist" means anyone using the transportation system
 4   who is not in a vehicle.
 5        (d)  "Pedestrian  fatality"  means  any  death  of  a  pedestrian
 6   resulting from a collision, whether on a roadway, at an intersection,
 7   along an adjacent sidewalk, or on a path that is contiguous with a
 8   roadway.
 9        (e) "Serious injury" means any injury other than a fatal injury
10   that prevents the injured person from walking, driving, or normally
11   continuing the activities the person was capable of performing before
12   the injury occurred.

                                            p. 95                          ESSB 5801.SL
 1        (6)(a) Documents prepared by or for the council are inadmissible
 2   and may not be used in a civil or administrative proceeding, except
 3   that any document that exists before its use or consideration in a
 4   review by the council, or that is created independently of such
 5   review, does not become inadmissible merely because it is reviewed or
 6   used by the council. For confidential information, such as personally
 7   identifiable information and medical records, which are obtained by
 8   the council, neither the commission nor the council may publicly
 9   disclose  such  confidential  information.  No  person  who  was  in
10   attendance at a meeting of the council or who participated in the
11   creation, retention, collection, or maintenance of information or
12   documents specifically for the commission or the council shall be
13   permitted to testify in any civil action as to the content of such
14   proceedings or of the documents and information prepared specifically
15   as part of the activities of the council. However, recommendations
16   from the council and the commission generally may be disclosed
17   without personal identifiers.
18        (b)  The  council  may  review,  only  to  the  extent  otherwise
19   permitted by law or court rule when determined to be relevant and
20   necessary:  Any  law  enforcement  incident  documentation,  such  as
21   incident reports, dispatch records, and victim, witness, and suspect
22   statements; any supplemental reports, probable cause statements, and
23   911 call taker's reports; and any other information determined to be
24   relevant to the review. The commission and the council must maintain
25   the confidentiality of such information to the extent required by any
26   applicable law.
27        (7) If acting in good faith, without malice, and within the
28   parameters  of  and  protocols  established  under  this  chapter,
29   representatives of the commission and the council are immune from
30   civil liability for an activity related to reviews of particular
31   fatalities and serious injuries.
32        (8) This section must not be construed to provide a private civil
33   cause of action.
34        (9)(a) The council may receive gifts, grants, or endowments from
35   public or private sources that are made from time to time, in trust
36   or otherwise, for the use and benefit of the purposes of the council
37   and spend the gifts, grants, or endowments from the public or private
38   sources according to their terms, unless the receipt of the gifts,
39   grants, or endowments violates RCW 29B.45.020.


                                            p. 96                          ESSB 5801.SL
 1        (b) Subject to the appropriation of funds for this specific
 2   purpose,  the  council  may  provide  grants  targeted  at  improving
 3   pedestrian, bicyclist, or nonmotorist safety in accordance with
 4   recommendations made by the council.
 5        (10) For purposes of this section:
 6        (a) "Bicyclist fatality" means any death of a bicyclist resulting
 7   from a collision, whether on a roadway, at an intersection, along an
 8   adjacent sidewalk, or on a path that is contiguous with a roadway.
 9        (b) "Council" means the Cooper Jones active transportation safety
10   council.
11        (c) "Nonmotorist" means anyone using the transportation system
12   who is not in a vehicle.
13        (d)  "Pedestrian  fatality"  means  any  death  of  a  pedestrian
14   resulting from a collision, whether on a roadway, at an intersection,
15   along an adjacent sidewalk, or on a path that is contiguous with a
16   roadway.
17        (e) "Serious injury" means any injury other than a fatal injury
18   that prevents the injured person from walking, driving, or normally
19   continuing the activities the person was capable of performing before
20   the injury occurred.


21                                     Shared Streets


22        Sec. 904.  RCW 46.61.--- and 2025 c . . . (ESB 5595) s 1 are each
23   amended to read as follows:
24        (1)(a) A local authority may designate a nonarterial highway,
25   except as provided in       (b) of this subsection, to be a shared street
26   under this section, if the local authority has developed procedures
27   for establishing shared streets.
28        (b) Nonarterial highways that are state highways may not be
29   designated shared streets unless they are the primary roads through a
30   central business district.          For the purposes of this subsection,
31   "central  business  district"  means  a  downtown  or  neighborhood
32   commercial area with boundaries defined in the local ordinance
33   designating the shared street. A local authority must consult with
34   the  department  of  transportation  and  obtain  the  department's
35   approval, consistent with the requirements of RCW              47.24.020, before
36   establishing a shared street on a state highway.



                                            p. 97                          ESSB 5801.SL
 1        (2) Vehicular traffic traveling along a shared street shall yield
 2   the right-of-way to any pedestrian, bicyclist, or operator of a
 3   micromobility device on the shared street.
 4        (3) A bicyclist or operator of a micromobility device shall yield
 5   the right-of-way to any pedestrian on a shared street.
 6        (4) Any local authority that designates a nonarterial highway to
 7   be a shared street as provided by this section must post an annual
 8   report on the local authority's website of the number of traffic
 9   accidents, including those that involve a pedestrian, bicyclist, or
10   operator of a micromobility device, that occurred on the designated
11   shared street. The report must also include the number of speeding
12   violations and driving under the influence violations that occurred
13   on the designated shared street.
14        (5) For purposes of this section:
15        (a) "Micromobility device" means personal or shared nonmotorized
16   scooters, "motorized foot scooters" as defined in RCW              46.04.336, and
17   "electric personal assistive mobility devices"            (EPAMD) as defined in
18   RCW 46.04.1695; and
19        (b) "Shared street" means a city street designated by placement
20   of official traffic control devices where pedestrians, bicyclists,
21   and vehicular traffic share a portion or all of the same street.


22                          Automated Traffic Safety Cameras


23        Sec. 905.  RCW 46.63.210 and 2024 c 307 s 1 are each amended to
24   read as follows:
25        The definitions in this section apply throughout this section and
26   RCW   46.63.220 through     46.63.260 unless the context clearly requires
27   otherwise.
28        (1) "Automated traffic safety camera" means a device that uses a
29   vehicle sensor installed to work in conjunction with an intersection
30   traffic control system, a railroad grade crossing control system, or
31   a speed measuring device, and a camera synchronized to automatically
32   record  one  or  more  sequenced  photographs,  microphotographs,  or
33   electronic images of the front or rear of a motor vehicle at the time
34   the vehicle fails to stop when facing a steady red traffic control
35   signal or an activated railroad grade crossing control signal, or
36   exceeds a speed limit as detected by a speed measuring device.
37   "Automated traffic safety camera" also includes a device used to
38   detect stopping at intersection or crosswalk violations; stopping
                                            p. 98                          ESSB 5801.SL
 1   when traffic obstructed violations; public transportation only lane
 2   violations; stopping or traveling in restricted lane violations; and
 3   public  transportation  bus  stop  zone  violations                  and  public
 4   transportation  only  lane  violations  detected  by  a  public
 5   transportation vehicle-mounted system.
 6        (2) "Hospital speed zone" means the marked area within hospital
 7   property and extending 300 feet from the border of the hospital
 8   property   (a) consistent with hospital use; and           (b) where signs are
 9   posted to indicate the location is within a hospital speed zone,
10   where "hospital" has the same meaning as in RCW 70.41.020.
11        (3) "Public park speed zone" means the marked area within public
12   park property and extending 300 feet from the border of the public
13   park property     (a) consistent with active park use; and              (b) where
14   signs are posted to indicate the location is within a public park
15   speed zone.
16        (4) "Public transportation vehicle" means any motor vehicle,
17   streetcar, train, trolley vehicle, ferry boat, or any other device,
18   vessel, or vehicle that is owned or operated by a transit authority
19   or an entity providing service on behalf of a transit authority that
20   is used for the purpose of carrying passengers and that operates on
21   established routes. "Transit authority" has the same meaning as
22   provided in RCW 9.91.025.
23        (5) "Roadway work zone" means an area of any city roadway,
24   including state highways that are also classified as city streets
25   under chapter 47.24 RCW, or county road as defined in RCW 46.04.150,
26   with construction, maintenance, or utility work with a duration of 30
27   calendar days or more. A roadway work zone is identified by the
28   placement of temporary traffic control devices that may include
29   signs, channelizing devices, barriers, pavement markings, and/or work
30   vehicles with warning lights. A roadway work zone extends from the
31   first warning sign or high intensity rotating, flashing, oscillating,
32   or strobe lights on a vehicle to the end road work sign or the last
33   temporary traffic control device or vehicle.
34        (6) "School speed zone" has the same meaning as described in RCW
35   46.61.440 (1) and (2).
36        (7) "School walk zone" means a roadway identified under RCW
37   28A.160.160 or roadways within a one-mile radius of a school that
38   students use to travel to school by foot, bicycle, or other means of
39   active transportation.

 1        Sec. 906.  RCW 46.63.220 and 2024 c 307 s 2 are each amended to
 2   read as follows:
 3        (1) Nothing in this section prohibits a law enforcement officer
 4   from issuing a notice of traffic infraction to a person in control of
 5   a vehicle at the time a violation occurs under RCW 46.63.030(1) (a),
 6   (b), or (c).
 7        (2) Any city or county may authorize the use of automated traffic
 8   safety cameras and must adopt an ordinance authorizing such use
 9   through its local legislative authority.
10        (3) The local legislative authority must prepare an analysis of
11   the locations within the jurisdiction where automated traffic safety
12   cameras are proposed to be located before adding traffic safety
13   cameras to a new location or relocating any existing camera to a new
14   location within the jurisdiction. The analysis must include equity
15   considerations including the impact of the camera placement on
16   livability, accessibility, economics, education, and environmental
17   health when identifying where to locate an automated traffic safety
18   camera. The analysis must also show a demonstrated need for traffic
19   cameras based on one or more of the following in the vicinity of the
20   proposed camera location: Travel by vulnerable road users, evidence
21   of vehicles speeding, rates of collision, reports showing near
22   collisions,   and   anticipated   or   actual   ineffectiveness   or
23   infeasibility of other mitigation measures.
24        (4) Automated traffic safety cameras may not be used on an on-
25   ramp to a limited access facility as defined in RCW 47.52.010.
26        (5) A city may use automated traffic safety cameras to enforce
27   traffic ordinances in this section on state highways that are also
28   classified as city streets under chapter 47.24 RCW. A city government
29   must notify the department of transportation when it installs an
30   automated traffic safety camera to enforce traffic ordinances as
31   authorized in this subsection.

33   section must contain the restrictions described in this section and

35   also post such restrictions and other automated traffic safety camera

32   used on roadways or intersections must be clearly marked by placing
33   signs at least 30 days prior to activation of the camera in locations
34   that clearly indicate to a driver either that:               (a) The driver is
35   within an area where automated traffic safety cameras are authorized;

                                           p. 101                         ESSB 5801.SL

 6   to enforce bus stop zone violations           and public transportation only
 7   lane violations.

 9   the vehicle and vehicle license plate and only while an infraction is
10   occurring. The image must not reveal the face of the driver or of
11   passengers in the vehicle. The primary purpose of camera placement is
12   to record images of the vehicle and vehicle license plate when an
13   infraction is occurring. Cities and counties must consider installing
14   automated traffic safety cameras in a manner that minimizes the
15   impact of camera flash on drivers.

17   of the vehicle within 14 days of the violation, or to the renter of a
18   vehicle within 14 days of establishing the renter's name and address
19   under subsection (17) of this section. The notice of infraction must
20   include with it a certificate or facsimile thereof, based upon
21   inspection of photographs, microphotographs, or electronic images
22   produced by an automated traffic safety camera, stating the facts
23   supporting the notice of infraction. This certificate or facsimile is
24   prima facie evidence of the facts contained in it and is admissible
25   in  a  proceeding  charging  a  violation  under  this  chapter.  The
26   photographs, microphotographs, or electronic images evidencing the
27   violation  must  be  available  for  inspection  and  admission  into
28   evidence  in  a  proceeding  to  adjudicate  the  liability  for  the
29   infraction. A person receiving a notice of infraction based on
30   evidence detected by an automated traffic safety camera may respond
31   to the notice by mail.

16        Sec. 907.  RCW 46.63.260 and 2024 c 307 s 6 are each amended to
17   read as follows:
18        (1)(a)  Subject  to  RCW         46.63.220  and  as  limited  in  this
19   subsection, automated traffic safety cameras may be used in cities
20   with populations of more than 500,000 residents to detect one or more
21   of the following violations:
22        (i) Stopping when traffic obstructed violations;
23        (ii) Stopping at intersection or crosswalk violations;
24        (iii) Public transportation only lane violations; or
25        (iv) Stopping or traveling in restricted lane violations.
26        (b) Use of automated traffic safety cameras as authorized in this
27   subsection     (1)  is  restricted  to  the  following  locations  only:
28   Intersections  as  described  in  RCW           46.63.230(2);  railroad  grade
29   crossings; school speed zones; school walk zones; public park speed
30   zones; hospital speed zones; and midblock on arterials. The use of
31   such automated traffic safety cameras is further limited to the
32   following:
33        (i) The portion of state and local roadways in downtown areas of
34   the city used for office and commercial activities, as well as retail
35   shopping and support services, and that may include mixed residential
36   uses;
37        (ii) The portion of state and local roadways in areas in the city
38   within one-half mile north of the boundary of the area described in
39   (b)(i) of this subsection;
                                            p. 107                         ESSB 5801.SL
 1        (iii) Portions of roadway systems in the city that travel into
 2   and out of     (b)(ii) of this subsection that are designated by the
 3   Washington  state  department  of  transportation  as  noninterstate
 4   freeways for up to four miles; and
 5        (iv) Portions of roadway systems in the city connected to the
 6   portions of the noninterstate freeways identified in (b)(iii) of this
 7   subsection that are designated by the Washington state department of
 8   transportation as arterial roadways for up to one mile from the
 9   intersection of the arterial roadway and the noninterstate freeway.
10        (2) Subject to RCW       46.63.220, automated traffic safety cameras
11   may also be used in cities with a bus rapid transit corridor or
12   routes to detect public transportation only lane violations.
13        (3) Subject to RCW       46.63.220, automated traffic safety cameras
14   that are part of a public transportation vehicle-mounted system may
15   be used by a transit authority within a county with a population of
16   more than 1,500,000 residents to detect stopping, standing, or
17   parking in bus stop zone violations or traveling, stopping, standing,
18   or parking in a public transportation only lane violations if
19   authorized by the local legislative authority with jurisdiction over
20   the transit authority.
21        (4) Subject to RCW         46.63.220, and in consultation with the
22   department of transportation, automated traffic safety cameras may be
23   used to detect ferry queue violations under RCW 46.61.735.
24        (5)  A  transit  authority  may  not  take  disciplinary  action
25   regarding a warning or infraction issued pursuant to subsections (1)
26   through (3) of this section against an employee who was operating a
27   public transportation vehicle at the time the violation that was the
28   basis of the warning or infraction was detected.


29                   PART X: PUBLIC TRANSPORTATION BENEFIT AREAS


30        NEW SECTION.       Sec. 1001.      A new section is added to          chapter
31   36.57A RCW to read as follows:
32        (1) A public transportation benefit area authority as provided in
33   subsection     (2) of this section may, pursuant to an interlocal
34   agreement, annex an adjacent city operating a transit system under
35   chapter     35.95  RCW  within  the  county  in  which  the  public
36   transportation benefit area is located. This method of annexation is
37   an alternative method and is additional to all other methods provided
38   for in this chapter.
                                           p. 108                         ESSB 5801.SL
 1        (2) An authority and the governing body of an adjacent city
 2   described in subsection       (1) of this section may jointly initiate an
 3   annexation  process  for  annexing  the  city  into  the  public
 4   transportation benefit area by adopting an interlocal agreement as
 5   provided in    chapter   39.34 RCW and under this subsection between the
 6   authority and the city. The authority and the city shall jointly
 7   agree on the annexation and its effective date. The interlocal
 8   agreement must set a date for a public hearing on the agreement for
 9   annexation.
10        (3) A public hearing must be held by each governing body,
11   separately  or  jointly,  before  the  agreement  is  executed.  Each
12   governing body holding a public hearing shall:
13        (a) Separately or jointly, publish a notice of availability of
14   the agreement at least once a week for four weeks before the date of
15   the hearing in one or more newspapers of general circulation within
16   the public transportation benefit area and one or more newspapers of
17   general circulation within the city; and
18        (b) If the governing body has the ability to do so, post the
19   notice of availability of the agreement on its website for the same
20   four weeks that the notice is published in the newspapers under                 (a)
21   of this subsection. The notice must describe where the public may
22   review the agreement.
23        (4) On the date set for hearing, the public must be afforded an
24   opportunity to be heard. Following the hearing, if the governing body
25   determines to undertake the annexation, it must do so by ordinance,
26   if  a  city's  governing  body,  and  by  resolution,  if  a  public
27   transportation benefit area's governing body. Upon the effective date
28   of the annexation the city annexed must become part of the public
29   transportation benefit area and must cease operating a transit system
30   under chapter 35.95 RCW. Upon passage of the annexation ordinance and
31   resolution  a  certified  copy  of  each  must  be  filed  with  the
32   legislative authority of the county in which the city is located.
33        (5) After an annexation under this section occurs, the county
34   legislative  authority  of  the  county  in  which  the  public
35   transportation benefit area is located may by resolution annex county
36   area under its jurisdiction into the public transportation benefit
37   area. This method of annexation is an alternative method and is
38   additional to all other methods provided for in this chapter.

19                  PART XII: PUBLIC-PRIVATE PARTNERSHIP PROJECTS


20        NEW SECTION.      Sec. 1201.     (1) The legislature finds that a full
21   set of project procurement, contracting, financing, and funding tools
22   are needed to enable the delivery of transportation projects in a
23   manner  most  advantageous  to  the  public.  Current  public-private
24   partnership laws have failed to spur innovative proposals from the
25   private sector or new project delivery approaches from the department
26   of transportation.
27        (2) The legislature confirms the findings from previous studies
28   that current laws and administrative processes are the primary
29   obstacle impairing the state's ability to utilize public-private
30   partnerships.  The  legislature  finds  that  a  new  public-private
31   partnership law is needed to:
32        (a) Transparently demonstrate and deliver better value for the
33   public including, but not limited to, expedited project delivery and
34   more effective management of project life-cycle costs;
35        (b)  Provide  an  additional  option  for  delivering  complex
36   transportation projects, including addressing a shortage of truck
37   parking;


                                           p. 114                         ESSB 5801.SL
 1        (c) Incorporate private sector expertise and innovation into
 2   transportation project delivery;
 3        (d) Allocate project risks to the parties best able to manage
 4   those risks;
 5        (e) Allow new sources of private capital;
 6        (f) Increase access to federal funding and financing mechanisms;
 7        (g)  Better  align  private  sector  incentives  with  public
 8   priorities; and
 9        (h) Provide consistency in the review and approval processes for
10   the full range of project delivery tools and contracting methods.


11        NEW SECTION.      Sec. 1202.      DEFINITIONS. The definitions in this
12   section apply throughout this chapter unless the context clearly
13   requires otherwise.
14        (1) "Commission" means the transportation commission.
15        (2) "Department" means the department of transportation.
16        (3) "Eligible transportation project" means any project that is
17   not a rail project and meets the criteria to be evaluated for
18   delivery in section 1206 of this act, whether capital or operating,
19   where  the  state's  purpose  for  the  project  is  to  preserve  or
20   facilitate the safe transport of people or goods via any mode of
21   travel.
22        (4) "Private sector partner" and "private partner" means a
23   person, entity, or organization that is not the federal government, a
24   state, or a political subdivision of a state.
25        (5) "Public funds" means all moneys derived from taxes, fees,
26   charges, tolls, or other levies of money from the public.
27        (6) "Public sector partner" and "public partner" means any
28   federal  or  state  unit  of  government,  bistate  transportation
29   organization, or any other political subdivision of any state.
30        (7) "State finance committee" means the entity created in chapter
31   43.33 RCW.
32        (8) "Unit of government" means any department or agency of the
33   federal government, any state or agency, office, or department of a
34   state, any city, county, district, commission, authority, entity,
35   port, or other public corporation organized and existing under
36   statutory law or under a voter-approved charter or initiative, and
37   any intergovernmental entity created under           chapter 39.34 RCW or this
38   chapter.


                                           p. 115                         ESSB 5801.SL
 1        NEW  SECTION.        Sec.  1203.       WASHINGTON  STATE  DEPARTMENT  OF
 2   TRANSPORTATION POWERS AND DUTIES.          (1) The department shall develop
 3   policies and, where appropriate, adopt rules to carry out this
 4   chapter  and  govern  the  use  of  public-private  partnerships  for
 5   transportation projects. At a minimum, the department's policies and
 6   rules must address the following issues:
 7        (a) The types of projects allowed;
 8        (b) Consistent with section 1209 of this act, a process and
 9   methodology for determining whether a public-private partnership
10   delivery model will be in the public's interest;
11        (c) Consistent with section 1214 of this act, a process and
12   methodology  for  determining  whether  a  negotiated  partnership
13   agreement will result in greater public value to the state than if
14   the project is delivered using other procurement and contracting
15   methods;
16        (d) The types of contracts allowed, with consideration given to
17   the best practices available;
18        (e) Minimum standards and criteria required of all proposals;
19        (f)  Procedures  for  the  proper  identification,  solicitation,
20   acceptance, review, and evaluation of projects, consistent with
21   existing  project  procurement  and  contracting  requirements  and
22   practices;
23        (g) Criteria to be considered in the evaluation and selection of
24   proposals that includes:
25        (i) Comparison with the department's internal ability to complete
26   the project that documents the advantages of completing the project
27   as a partnership versus solely as a public venture; and
28        (ii) Factors such as, but not limited to: Priority, life-cycle
29   cost,  risk  sharing,  scheduling,  innovation,  and  management
30   conditions;
31        (h) The protection of confidential proprietary information while
32   still meeting the need for transparency and public disclosure that is
33   consistent with section 1215 of this act;
34        (i)  Protection  for  local  contractors  to  participate  in
35   subcontracting opportunities that is consistent with section 1204(3)
36   of this act;
37        (j) Specifying that maintenance issues must be resolved in a
38   manner consistent with chapter 41.80 RCW;
39        (k) Guidelines to address security and performance issues.


                                            p. 116                         ESSB 5801.SL
 1        (2)  During  its  rule-making  activities,  the  department  must
 2   consult with the department's office of equity and civil rights.
 3        (3) By September 1, 2026, the department must provide a report to
 4   the house of representatives and senate transportation committees on
 5   proposed  policies  and  guidelines  it  intends  to  develop  into
 6   administrative rules. Rules adopted by the department pursuant to
 7   this chapter may not take effect before January 1, 2027.


 8        NEW SECTION.      Sec. 1204.      APPLICABILITY OF OTHER TRANSPORTATION
 9   PROJECT GOVERNING PROVISIONS.          (1) For any eligible transportation
10   project that requires the imposition of tolls on a state facility,
11   the legislature must approve the imposition of such tolls consistent
12   with RCW 47.56.820.
13        (2) For any eligible transportation project that requires setting
14   or adjusting toll rates on a state facility, the commission has sole
15   responsibility consistent with RCW 47.56.850.
16        (3)(a)  If  federal  funds  are  provided  for  an  eligible
17   transportation project developed under this chapter, disadvantaged
18   business   enterprise   inclusion   requirements,   as   established,
19   monitored, and administered by the department's office of equity and
20   civil rights, apply.
21        (b)  If  no  federal  funds  are  provided  for  an  eligible
22   transportation project developed under this chapter, state laws,
23   rates, and rules must govern, including the public works small
24   business  certification  program  pursuant  to  RCW              39.19.030(7)  as
25   monitored and administered by the department's office of equity and
26   civil rights.


27        NEW SECTION.  Sec. 1205.  APPLICATION OF PUBLIC WORKS PROVISIONS.
28   All other transportation and public works project procurement and
29   contracting governing provisions and procedures that do not conflict
30   with this chapter, including responsible bidder and apprenticeship
31   requirements under chapter 39.04 RCW and prevailing wage requirements
32   under   chapter   39.12 RCW, apply to the entire eligible transportation
33   project.


34        NEW  SECTION.        Sec.  1206.       PROJECT  COST  THRESHOLD  FOR  P3
35   EVALUATION. Any eligible transportation project with an estimated
36   cost to the state of less than $500,000,000, or any project on a
37   United States route that is not an interstate route and includes
                                            p. 117                         ESSB 5801.SL
 1   replacement of a seismically vulnerable elevated structure at least
 2   one and one-half miles long that crosses a river, may be evaluated
 3   for delivery under a public-private partnership model as prescribed
 4   under this chapter.


 5        NEW SECTION.  Sec. 1207.  ELIGIBLE FINANCING.            (1) Subject to the
 6   limitations in this section, the department may, in connection with
 7   the evaluation of eligible transportation projects, consider any
 8   financing mechanisms from any lawful source, either integrated as
 9   part of a project proposal or as a separate, stand-alone proposal to
10   finance a project. Financing may be considered for all or part of a
11   proposed project. A project may be financed in whole or in part with:
12        (a) The proceeds of grant anticipation revenue bonds authorized
13   under 23 U.S.C. Sec. 122 and applicable state law. Legislative
14   authorization and appropriation are required to use this source of
15   financing;
16        (b) Grants, loans, loan guarantees, lines of credit, revolving
17   lines of credit, or other financing arrangements available under the
18   transportation infrastructure finance and innovation act under 23
19   U.S.C. Sec. 181 et seq., or any other applicable federal law, subject
20   to legislative authorization and appropriation as required;
21        (c)  Infrastructure  loans  or  assistance  from  the  state
22   infrastructure bank established under RCW                82.44.195, subject to
23   legislative authorization and appropriation as required;
24        (d) Federal, state, or local revenues, subject to appropriation
25   by the applicable legislative authority;
26        (e) User fees, tolls, fares, lease proceeds, rents, gross or net
27   receipts from sales, proceeds from the sale of development rights,
28   franchise fees, or any other lawful form of consideration. However,
29   projects  financed  by  tolls  must  first  be  authorized  by  the
30   legislature under RCW 47.56.820;
31        (f) Loans, pledges, or contributions of funds, including equity
32   investments, from private entities;
33        (g) Revenue bonds, subject to legislative authorization and
34   appropriation as required.
35        (2) Subject to subsection (4) of this section, the department may
36   develop a plan of finance that would require either the state or a
37   private partner, or both, to: Issue debt, equity, or other securities
38   or obligations; enter into contracts, leases, concessions, and grant
39   and loan agreements; or secure any financing with a pledge of funds
                                            p. 118                         ESSB 5801.SL
 1   to be appropriated by the legislature or with a lien or exchange of
 2   real property.
 3        (3) As security for the payment of any financing, the revenues
 4   from the project may be pledged, but no such pledge of revenues
 5   constitutes in any manner or to any extent a general obligation of
 6   the state, unless specifically authorized by the legislature. Any
 7   financing described in this section may be structured on a senior,
 8   parity, or subordinate basis to any other financing.
 9        (4) The department shall not execute any agreement with respect
10   to an eligible transportation project, including any agreement that
11   could materially impact the state's debt capacity or credit rating as
12   determined by the state finance committee, without prior review and
13   approval of the plan of finance and proposed financing terms by the
14   state finance committee.


15        NEW SECTION.  Sec. 1208.        USE OF FEDERAL FUNDS OR OTHER SOURCES.
16   (1) The department may accept from the United States or any of its
17   agencies such funds as are available to this state or to any other
18   unit of government for carrying out the purposes of this chapter,
19   whether the funds are made available by grant, loan, or other
20   financing arrangement. The department may enter into such agreements
21   and other arrangements with the United States or any of its agencies
22   as may be necessary, proper, and convenient for carrying out the
23   purposes of this chapter, subject to subsection (2) of this section.
24        (2)(a) The department may accept from any source any grant,
25   donation, gift, or other form of conveyance of land, money, other
26   real or personal property, or other valuable thing made to the state
27   of Washington, the department, or a local government for carrying out
28   the purposes of this chapter.
29        (b) Any eligible transportation project may be financed in whole
30   or in part by contribution of any funds or property made by any
31   private entity or public sector partner that is a party to any
32   agreement entered into under this chapter.


33        NEW SECTION.       Sec. 1209.       PUBLIC INTEREST FINDING.         (1) The
34   department may evaluate eligible transportation projects that are
35   already programmed for other delivery methods to determine their
36   appropriateness  for  delivery  under  a  public-private  partnership
37   model.


                                           p. 119                         ESSB 5801.SL
 1        (2) Before entering into a formal solicitation or procurement to
 2   develop a project as a public-private partnership, the department
 3   must make formal findings that utilizing a public-private partnership
 4   delivery method is in the public's interest. The department must
 5   adopt rules detailing the process and criteria for making such
 6   findings. At a minimum, the criteria must consider whether:
 7        (a) Public ownership of the asset can be retained;
 8        (b) Transparency during the consideration of a public-private
 9   partnership agreement can be provided;
10        (c) Public oversight of the private entity's management of the
11   asset can be provided; and
12        (d) Additional criteria that reflects the legislative findings in
13   section 1201 of this act.
14        (3) Before commencing any solicitation to deliver the project as
15   a  public-private  partnership,  the  department  must  provide  an
16   opportunity for public comment on the proposed project and delivery
17   method.
18        (4) Upon a finding of public interest pursuant to subsection (2)
19   of this section, the department must provide written notification of
20   their finding of public interest and intent to deliver the project as
21   a public-private partnership to the general public, to the chairs and
22   ranking members of the transportation committees of the legislature,
23   and to the governor.
24        (5) Upon a finding of public interest pursuant to subsection (2)
25   of this section, the department may:
26        (a) Solicit concepts or proposals for the identified public-
27   private partnership project from private entities and units of
28   government;
29        (b) Evaluate the concepts or proposals received under this
30   section.  The  evaluation  under  this  subsection  must  include
31   consultation with any appropriate unit of government; and
32        (c) Select potential projects based on the concepts or proposals.


33        NEW SECTION.  Sec. 1210.  USE OF FUNDS FOR PROPOSAL PURPOSES. (1)
34   Subject to the availability of amounts appropriated for this specific
35   purpose, the department may spend such moneys as may be necessary for
36   stipends  for  respondents  to  a  solicitation,  the  evaluation  of
37   concepts or proposals for eligible transportation projects, and for
38   negotiating   agreements   for   eligible   transportation   projects
39   authorized under this chapter. Expenses incurred by the department
                                            p. 120                         ESSB 5801.SL
 1   under this section before the issuance of transportation project
 2   bonds or other financing must be paid by the department and charged
 3   to the appropriate project. The department must keep records and
 4   accounts showing each charged amount.
 5        (2) Unless otherwise provided in the omnibus transportation
 6   appropriations act, the funds spent by the department under this
 7   section in connection with the project must be repaid from the
 8   proceeds  of  the  bonds  or  other  financing  upon  the  sale  of
 9   transportation project bonds or upon obtaining other financing for an
10   eligible transportation project, as allowed by law or contract.


11        NEW SECTION.  Sec. 1211.  EXPERT CONSULTATION. The department may
12   consult with legal, financial, technical, and other experts in the
13   public  and  private  sector  in  the  evaluation,  negotiation,  and
14   development of projects under this chapter.


15        NEW SECTION.      Sec. 1212.     CONTRACTED STUDIES. In the absence of
16   any direct federal funding or direction, the department may contract
17   with a private developer of a selected project proposal to conduct
18   environmental impact studies and engineering and technical studies.


19        NEW SECTION.        Sec. 1213.        PARTNERSHIP AGREEMENTS.        (1) The
20   following provisions must be included in any transportation project
21   agreement entered into under the authority of this chapter and to
22   which the state is a party:
23        (a)  For  any  project  that  proposes  terms  for  stand  alone
24   maintenance or asset management services for a public facility, those
25   services must be provided in a manner consistent with any collective
26   bargaining agreements, chapter 41.80 RCW, and civil service laws that
27   are in effect for the public facility;
28        (b) A finding of public interest, as issued by the department
29   pursuant to section 1209 of this act;
30        (c) If there is a tolling component to the project, it must be
31   specified that the tolling technology used in the project must be
32   consistent  with  tolling  technology  standards  adopted  by  the
33   department for transportation-related projects;
34        (d) Provisions for bonding, financial guarantees, deposits, or
35   the posting of other security to secure the payment of laborers,
36   subcontractors, and suppliers who perform work or provide materials
37   as part of the project;
                                           p. 121                          ESSB 5801.SL
 1        (e) All projects must be financed in a manner consistent with
 2   section 1207 of this act.
 3        (2) At a minimum, agreements between the state and private sector
 4   partners entered into under this section must specifically include
 5   the following contractual elements:
 6        (a) The point in the project at which public and private sector
 7   partners will enter the project and which partners will assume
 8   responsibility for specific project elements;
 9        (b) How the partners will share management of the risks of the
10   project;
11        (c) The compensation method and amount for the private partner,
12   establishing a maximum rate of return, and identifying how project
13   revenue, if any, in excess of the maximum rate of return will be
14   distributed;
15        (d) How the partners will share the costs of development of the
16   project;
17        (e) How the partners will allocate financial responsibility for
18   cost overruns;
19        (f) The penalties for nonperformance;
20        (g) The incentives for performance;
21        (h) The accounting and auditing standards to be used to evaluate
22   work on the project;
23        (i) For any project that reverts to public ownership, the
24   responsibility for reconstruction or renovations that are required
25   for a facility to meet all service standards and state of good repair
26   upon reversion of the facility to the state;
27        (j) Provisions and remedies for default by either party, and
28   provisions for termination of the agreement for or without cause;
29        (k) Provisions for public communication and participation with
30   respect to the development of the project.


31        NEW SECTION.        Sec. 1214.       BEST VALUE FINDING AND AGREEMENT
32   EXECUTION. Before executing an agreement under section 1213 of this
33   act, the department must make a formal finding that the negotiated
34   partnership agreement is expected to result in best value for the
35   public, and the agreement must be approved through duly enacted
36   legislation. The department must develop and adopt a process and
37   criteria for measuring, determining, and transparently reporting best
38   value relevant to the proposed project. At minimum, the criteria must
39   include:
                                           p. 122                         ESSB 5801.SL
 1        (1) A comparison of the total cost to deliver the project,
 2   including any operations and maintenance costs, as a public-private
 3   partnership compared to traditional or other alternative delivery
 4   methods available to the department;
 5        (2) A comparison with the department's current plan, resources,
 6   delivery  capacity,  and  schedule  to  complete  the  project  that
 7   documents the advantages of completing the project as a public-
 8   private partnership versus solely as a public venture; and
 9        (3) Factors such as, but not limited to: Priority, cost, risk
10   sharing, scheduling, asset and service quality, innovation, and
11   management conditions.


12        NEW SECTION.        Sec. 1215.       CONFIDENTIALITY. A proposer must
13   identify those portions of a proposal that the proposer considers to
14   be  confidential,  proprietary  information,  or  trade  secrets  and
15   provide any justification as to why these materials, upon request,
16   should not be disclosed by the department. Patent information will be
17   covered  until  the  patent  expires.  Other  information,  such  as
18   originality of design or records of negotiation, is protected under
19   this section only until an agreement under section 1214 of this act
20   is  reached.  Eligible  transportation  projects  under  federal
21   jurisdiction  or  using  federal  funds  must  conform  to  federal
22   regulations under the freedom of information act.


23        NEW SECTION.      Sec. 1216.     GOVERNMENT AGREEMENTS. The state may,
24   either separately or in combination with any other public sector
25   partner, enter into working agreements, coordination agreements, or
26   similar implementation agreements, including the formation of bistate
27   transportation organizations, to carry out the joint implementation
28   and operation of an eligible transportation project selected under
29   this chapter. The state may enter into agreements with other units of
30   government  or  Canadian  provinces  for  transborder  transportation
31   projects.


32        NEW SECTION.  Sec. 1217.  EMINENT DOMAIN. The state may exercise
33   the power of eminent domain to acquire property, easements, or other
34   rights or interests in property for projects that are necessary to
35   implement an eligible transportation project developed under this
36   chapter. Any property acquired pursuant to this section must be owned
37   in fee simple by the state.
                                           p. 123                          ESSB 5801.SL
 1        NEW SECTION.      Sec. 1218.  FEDERAL LAWS. Applicable federal laws,
 2   rules, and regulations govern in any situation that involves federal
 3   funds if the federal laws, rules, or regulations:
 4        (1) Conflict with any provision of this chapter;
 5        (2) Require procedures that are additional to or inconsistent
 6   with those provided in this chapter; or
 7        (3) Require contract provisions not authorized in this chapter.


 8        NEW SECTION.       Sec. 1219.      PUBLIC-PRIVATE PARTNERSHIPS ACCOUNT.
 9   (1) The public-private partnerships account is created in the custody
10   of the state treasurer.
11        (2) The following moneys must be deposited into the account:
12        (a) Proceeds from bonds or other financing instruments;
13        (b) Revenues received from any transportation project developed
14   under this chapter or developed under the general powers granted to
15   the department; and
16        (c) Any other moneys that are by donation, grant, contract, law,
17   or  other  means  transferred,  allocated,  or  appropriated  to  the
18   account.
19        (3) Expenditures from the account may be used only for the
20   planning, acquisition, financing, development, design, construction,
21   reconstruction, replacement, improvement, maintenance, preservation,
22   management, repair, or operation of any eligible transportation
23   project under this chapter.
24        (4) The state treasurer may establish separate subaccounts within
25   the  public-private  partnerships  account  for  each  transportation
26   project that is initiated under this chapter or under the general
27   powers granted to the department. The state may pledge moneys in the
28   public-private partnerships account to secure revenue bonds or any
29   other debt obligations relating to the project for which the account
30   is established.
31        (5) Only the secretary or the secretary's designee may authorize
32   distributions from the account. The account is subject to the
33   allotment procedures under chapter 43.88 RCW, but an appropriation is
34   not required for expenditures.


35        Sec. 1220.  RCW 47.56.030 and 2023 c 429 s 6 are each amended to

37        (1) Except as permitted under chapter            ((47.29))   47.--- RCW (the

                                            p. 125                         ESSB 5801.SL
 1        (2)  The  department  shall  proceed  with  the  procurement  of
 2   materials, supplies, services, and equipment needed for the support,
 3   maintenance, and use of a ferry, ferry terminal, or other facility
 4   operated by Washington state ferries, in accordance with                   chapter
 5   43.19 RCW except as follows:
 6        (a) When the secretary of the department of transportation
 7   determines in writing that the use of invitation for bid is either
 8   not practicable or not advantageous to the state and it may be
 9   necessary to make competitive evaluations, including technical or
10   performance evaluations among acceptable proposals to complete the
11   contract  award,  a  contract  may  be  entered  into  by  use  of  a
12   competitive  sealed  proposals  method,  and  a  formal  request  for
13   proposals   solicitation.   Such   formal   request   for   proposals
14   solicitation shall include a functional description of the needs and
15   requirements of the state and the significant factors.
16        (b)  When  purchases  are  made  through  a  formal  request  for
17   proposals  solicitation  the  contract  shall  be  awarded  to  the
18   responsible proposer whose competitive sealed proposal is determined
19   in writing to be the most advantageous to the state taking into
20   consideration price and other evaluation factors set forth in the
21   request  for  proposals.  No  significant  factors  may  be  used  in
22   evaluating a proposal that are not specified in the request for
23   proposals. Factors that may be considered in evaluating proposals
24   include but are not limited to: Price; maintainability; reliability;
25   commonality; performance levels; life-cycle cost if applicable under
26   this section; cost of transportation or delivery; delivery schedule
27   offered; installation cost; cost of spare parts; availability of
28   parts and service offered; and the following:
29        (i) The ability, capacity, and skill of the proposer to perform
30   the contract or provide the service required;
31        (ii) The character, integrity, reputation, judgment, experience,
32   and efficiency of the proposer;
33        (iii) Whether the proposer can perform the contract within the
34   time specified;
35        (iv)  The  quality  of  performance  of  previous  contracts  or
36   services;
37        (v) The previous and existing compliance by the proposer with
38   laws relating to the contract or services;
39        (vi) Objective, measurable criteria defined in the request for
40   proposal. These criteria may include but are not limited to items
                                           p. 126                         ESSB 5801.SL
 1   such  as  discounts,  delivery  costs,  maintenance  services  costs,
 2   installation costs, and transportation costs; and
 3        (vii) Such other information as may be secured having a bearing
 4   on the decision to award the contract.
 5        (c) When purchases are made through a request for proposal
 6   process,  proposals  received  shall  be  evaluated  based  on  the
 7   evaluation factors set forth in the request for proposal. When
 8   issuing a request for proposal for the procurement of propulsion
 9   equipment or systems that include an engine, the request for proposal
10   must specify the use of a life-cycle cost analysis that includes an
11   evaluation of fuel efficiency. When a life-cycle cost analysis is
12   used, the life-cycle cost of a proposal shall be given at least the
13   same relative importance as the initial price element specified in
14   the request of proposal documents. The department may reject any and
15   all proposals received. If the proposals are not rejected, the award
16   shall be made to the proposer whose proposal is most advantageous to
17   the department, considering price and the other evaluation factors
18   set forth in the request for proposal.

 4        Sec. 1302.  RCW 46.16A.305 and 2022 c 132 s 5 are each amended to
 5   read as follows:
 6        (1) The department, county auditor or other agent, or subagent
 7   appointed by the director may grant a temporary license plate to
 8   operate a vehicle for which an application for registration has been
 9   made. The application for a temporary license plate must be made by
10   the owner or the owner's representative to the department, county
11   auditor or other agent, or subagent appointed by the director on a
12   form furnished by the department and must contain:
13        (a) A full description of the vehicle, including its make, model,
14   vehicle identification number, and type of body;
15        (b) The name and address of the applicant;
16        (c) The date of application; and
17        (d) Other information that the department may require.
18        (2) Temporary license plates must:
19        (a) Be consecutively numbered;
20        (b) Be displayed as described for permanent license plates in RCW
21   46.16A.200(5)(a);
22        (c) Be composed of material that must be durable and remain
23   unaltered in field conditions for a minimum of four months; and
24        (d) Remain on the vehicle only until the receipt of permanent
25   license plates.
26        (3) The application must be accompanied by the fee required under
27   RCW 46.17.400(1)(b).
28        (4) Pursuant to subsection         (2) of this section, the department
29   may adopt rules for the design and display of temporary license
30   plates.
31        (5)   By  December  1,  2025,  the  department  must  adopt  rules
32   implementing  contingency  extensions  of  the  expiration  date  for
33   department  temporary  license  plates  in  cases  of  shortages  of
34   permanent license plates. The rules must prioritize reducing customer
35   return trips for department temporary license plates, and include a
36   communication plan with state and local law enforcement agencies
37   regarding the implementation of the contingency extensions.

 1        NEW SECTION.  Sec. 1303.  A new section is added to chapter 72.60
 2   RCW to read as follows:
 3        When the department of corrections, in conjunction with the
 4   department  of  licensing,  anticipates  a  projected  license  plate
 5   shortage statewide or in particular locations, the department of
 6   licensing must promptly communicate such shortage to the county
 7   auditors or other agents, and subagents appointed by the director of
 8   the department of licensing. The department of corrections, in
 9   conjunction with the department of licensing, must also develop and

14   period. Use of a third-party vendor may thereafter be initiated by

16   jointly by the two agencies.

18        Sec. 1304.     RCW 82.42.090 and 2017 3rd sp.s. c 25 s 42 are each
19   amended to read as follows:
20        All moneys collected by the director from the aircraft fuel
21   excise tax as provided in RCW         82.42.020 shall be transmitted to the
22   state treasurer and shall be credited to the aeronautics account
23   hereby created in the state treasury.           Moneys in the account may be
24   spent only after appropriation. Expenditures from the account may be
25   used only for aviation-related purposes. Moneys collected from the
26   consumer or user of aircraft fuel from either the use tax imposed by
27   RCW 82.12.020 or the retail sales tax imposed by RCW             82.08.020 shall
28   be transmitted to the state treasurer and credited to the state
29   general fund.

33        The jurisdiction, control, and duty of the state and city or town
34   with respect to such streets is as follows:

                                           p. 133                         ESSB 5801.SL

30   meet or exceed requirements established by the department;

32   overwidth permits for vehicles to operate on such streets or roads

                                           p. 134                         ESSB 5801.SL

                                           p. 135                         ESSB 5801.SL
 1   state or at the expense of the city or town or at the expense of the

29          Solicited Property Transactions For Transportation Purposes


30        Sec. 1306.      RCW 61.--.--- and 2025 c . . . (SHB 1081) s 1 are
31   each amended to read as follows:
32        (1) For real estate transactions executed on or after January 1,
33   2026, in which a potential buyer or someone representing a potential
34   buyer actively solicits the purchase of real property through public
35   advertising or written, electronic, or in-person contact with an
36   owner of real property that is not currently publicly available or
37   listed on the real estate market for purchase, the owner of the
38   solicited real property shall, upon execution of a purchase contract
                                            p. 136                         ESSB 5801.SL
 1   between the potential buyer and the owner of the solicited real
 2   property:
 3        (a) Have the right to an appraisal of the real property by an
 4   appraiser licensed in accordance with chapter 18.140 RCW, which right
 5   shall be expressly included in the purchase contract between the
 6   potential buyer and the owner of the solicited real property; and
 7        (b) Have the right to cancel the purchase contract without
 8   penalty or further obligation subject to subsection                  (2) of this
 9   section.
10        (2)(a) For owners of the solicited real property who wish to
11   exercise their right to an appraisal:
12        (i) The owner has the right to select the appraiser, and the
13   potential buyer is responsible for the expense of the appraisal;
14        (ii) The appraisal must be ordered within three business days
15   after the execution of the purchase contract, and the owner of the
16   solicited real property shall notify the potential buyer of the
17   appraisal; and
18        (iii) The owner of the solicited real property has the right to
19   cancel the purchase contract, without penalty or further obligation,
20   within four business days after the appraisal is received.
21        (b) For owners of solicited real property who do not wish to
22   receive an appraisal, the owner has the right to cancel the purchase
23   contract without penalty or further obligation within 10 business
24   days after execution of the contract.
25        (c) In the event of cancellation, the owner of the solicited real
26   property shall send a notice of cancellation to the buyer by mail,
27   telegram, email, or other means of written communication. Notice of
28   cancellation  is  considered  given  when  mailed,  when  filed  for
29   telegraphic transmission, when emailed, or, if sent by other means,
30   when delivered to the buyer's designated place of business.
31        (3) The purchase contract for a real estate transaction described
32   in this section must state clearly in at least size 10-point boldface
33   type, and the seller must affirmatively acknowledge in writing, that
34   the seller:
35        (a) Has a right to an appraisal as specified in subsection (2) of
36   this section; and
37        (b) Has a right to cancel the purchase contract without penalty
38   or further obligation in accordance with subsection                  (2) of this
39   section.


                                           p. 137                         ESSB 5801.SL
 1        (4) This section does not apply to a buyer or seller represented
 2   by a real estate broker licensed in accordance with                 chapter   18.85
 3   RCW.

 5   party as set forth in RCW 64.04.220.
 6        (6) The legislature finds that the practices covered by this
 7   section are matters vitally affecting the public interest for the
 8   purpose of applying the consumer protection act, chapter 19.86 RCW. A
 9   violation of this section is not reasonable in relation to the
